\frac{\log N({\cal U}_{F_{j}}|\pi)}{|F_{j}|}\leq\frac{\log N({\cal U}_{F_{i}}|\pi)}{|F_{i}|}+\varepsilon,\ \ \text{for all $\bar{\imath}\leq i,j\in I$.}\qed

4.2
Even covers and their properties

For the rest of this section, fix a compact group
K
with a right
G
-action
K\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 7.0pt$\rho$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowleft\ $}}G
and a closed
S
-invariant subgroup
H\leq K
. Necessarily,
\rho_{g}(H)=H
for every
g\in G
, since
\rho_{g}(H)\leq H
and
\rho_{g^{-1}}(H)\leq H
for every
g\in G
implies
H\leq\rho_{g}(H)
for every
g\in G
.
Denote by
\pi\colon K\to K/H
the canonical projection on the space of left cosets
K/H
, and by
H\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 7.0pt$\rho_{H}$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowleft\ $}}\ G
and
K/H\hskip 5.0pt\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{6.02773pt}{\hskip-2.5pt$\rho_{K/H}$}}}{\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{$\ \curvearrowleft\ $}}\hskip 6.0ptG
the induced right
G
-actions on
H
and
K/H
, respectively.

An open cover
{\cal U}\in\mathrm{cov}(K)
is said to be (
left
)
even
if
{\cal U}=\{xU:x\in K\}
for some open neighborhood
U
of
1\in K
. Let

{\cal U}_{K}[U]=\{xU:x\in K\}\quad\text{and}\quad{\cal U}_{K/H}[U]=\{\pi(xU):x\in K\}

(4.3)

the even cover of
K
associated with
U
and the even cover of
K/H
associated with
U
, respectively. When it is clear from the context, we simply write
{\cal U}[U]
instead of
{\cal U}_{K}[U]
.

First, we see that for even covers
{\cal U}\in\mathrm{cov}(K)
the quantity
N({\cal U}|\pi)
introduced in §
4.1
has a simple and clear meaning with respect to
H
:

Lemma 4.5
.

In the above setting, let
{\cal U}={\cal U}[V]\in\mathrm{cov}(K)
be an even cover and
F\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{fin}}(G)
. Then,
N({\cal U}_{F}|\pi)=N_{H}({\cal U}_{F})
.

Proof.

Recall that
N({\cal U}|\pi)=\sup_{k\in K}N_{kH}({\cal U})
, so it suffices to prove that
N_{kH}({\cal U}_{F})\leq N_{H}({\cal U}_{F})
for all
k\in K
.

Fix
k\in K
. Let
F=\{g_{1},\ldots,g_{n}\}
and
\rho_{m}=\rho_{g_{m}}
for every
m=1,\ldots,n
. Assume that

{\cal V}=\{V_{j}:j=1,\ldots,\ell\},\quad\text{with}\ V_{j}=\rho_{1}^{-1}(x_{j,1}V)\cap\ldots\cap\rho_{n}^{-1}(x_{j,n}V)\ \mbox{ and }\ x_{j,1},\ldots,x_{j,n}\in K,

is a finite subset of
{\cal U}_{F}
with
H\subseteq\bigcup{\cal V}=V_{1}\cup\ldots\cup V_{\ell}
and
N_{H}({\cal U}_{F})=|{\cal V}|=\ell
.
The containment
H\subseteq\bigcup{\cal V}
implies that

kH\subseteq k\bigcup{\cal V}=k(V_{1}\cup\ldots\cup V_{\ell})=kV_{1}\cup\ldots\cup kV_{\ell}=\bigcup k{\cal V}.

Now, for each
m=1,\ldots,n
, let
k_{m}=\rho_{m}(k)
, so that
k=\rho_{m}^{-1}(k_{m})
and hence, for every
j=1,\ldots,\ell
,

kV_{j}=k(\rho_{1}^{-1}(x_{j,1}V)\cap\ldots\cap\rho_{n}^{-1}(x_{j,n}V))=k\rho_{1}^{-1}(x_{j,1}V)\cap\ldots\cap k\rho_{n}^{-1}(x_{j,n}V)=\rho_{1}^{-1}(k_{1}x_{j,1}V)\cap\ldots\cap\rho_{n}^{-1}(k_{n}x_{j,n}V)=W_{j}.

Let
{\cal W}=\{W_{j}:j=1,\ldots,\ell\}
; then
k{\cal V}={\cal W}\subseteq{\cal U}_{F}
,
kH\subseteq\bigcup{\cal W}
and
|{\cal W}|\leq\ell=N_{H}({\cal U}_{F})
, so
N_{kH}({\cal U}_{F})\leq N_{H}({\cal U}_{F})
.
∎

Furthermore, extending the notation from Example
1.24
, for any subset
V
of
K/H
and
F\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{fin}}(G)
, we let

C_{F}(\rho_{K/H},V)=\bigcap_{f\in F}(\rho_{K/H})_{f}^{-1}(V)=T_{F}((\rho_{K/H})_{\mathfrak{U}},V).

In the sequel we use the fact that every compact group is SIN (i.e., has small invariant neighborhoods) in the sense of the following:

Fact 4.6

(
[
31
, Corollary 1.12]
)
.

Every compact group
K
has a local base of invariant (under conjugation) neighborhoods of
1
,
i.e.,
x^{-1}Vx\subseteq V
for every
x\in K
.

If the neighborhood
V
of
1\in K
is invariant, then
xV=Vx
for every
x\in K
and
C_{F}(\rho,V)
is invariant for every
F\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{fin}}(G)
.

Lemma 4.7
.

In the above setting, the following statements hold true:

(1)

each
{\cal U}\in\mathrm{cov}(K/H)
has an even refinement, that is,
{\cal U}\preceq{\cal U}_{K/H}[U]
for some open neighborhood
U
of
1\in K
(in particular,
N({\cal U})\leq N({\cal U}_{K/H}[U])
);

(2)

for each open neighborhood
U
of
1\in K
and
F\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{fin}}(G)
, we have
{\cal U}_{K/H}[C_{F}(\rho,U)]\subseteq T_{F}((\rho_{K/H})_{\mathrm{cov}},{\cal U}_{K/H}[U])
(in particular,
N(T_{F}((\rho_{K/H})_{\mathrm{cov}},{\cal U}_{K/H}[U]))\leq N({\cal U}_{K/H}[C_{F}(\rho,U)])
);

(3)

given two open neighborhoods
U
and
V
of
\ 1\in K
with
V^{-1}V\subseteq U
, and
F\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{fin}}(G)
, then
T_{F}((\rho_{K})_{\mathrm{cov}},{\cal U}_{K}[V])
refines
{\cal U}_{K}[C_{F}(\rho,U)]
(in particular,
N({\cal U}_{K}[C_{F}(\rho,U)])\leq N(T_{F}((\rho_{K})_{\mathrm{cov}},{\cal U}_{K}[V]))
);

(4)

given two open neighborhoods
U
and
V
of
1\in K
such that
V=V^{-1}
is invariant and
VV\subseteq U
, and
F\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{fin}}(G)
, let
D
be a subset of
K
which is maximal with respect to the following property:

(
*
)

\pi(d_{1}C_{F}(\rho,V))\cap\pi(d_{2}C_{F}(\rho,V))=\emptyset
, for all
d_{1}\neq d_{2}
in
D
.

Then,
\{\pi(dC_{F}(\rho,U)):d\in D\}
is a subcover of
{\cal U}_{K/H}[C_{F}(\rho,U)]
, so that
N({\cal U}_{K/H}[C_{F}(\rho,U)])\leq|D|
.

Proof.

(1) follows directly from a general version of the Lebesgue Covering Lemma (see
[
34
, Theorem 27]
).

(2) Here we use the left action
\tau
of
K
on itself by left traslations
{}_{x}t\colon k\mapsto xk
and the induced left action
\bar{\tau}
of
K
on
K/H
by left traslations
{}_{x}\bar{t}\colon kH\mapsto xkH
. For
A\subseteq K/H
, we shall briefly write
xA
in place of
{}_{x}\bar{t}(A)
. Since the projection
\pi\colon K\to K/H
is
K
-equivariant, for
x\in K
and
B\subseteq K
one has
x\pi(B)=\pi(xB)=\pi(x)\pi(B)
. Now it only remains to note that, for every
x\in K
,

\displaystyle\pi(xC_{F}(\rho,U))

\displaystyle=x\pi(C_{F}(\rho,U))=x\pi\left(\bigcap_{f\in F}\rho_{f}^{-1}(U)\right)\subseteq x\bigcap_{f\in F}\pi(\rho_{f}^{-1}(U))=\bigcap_{f\in F}x\pi(\rho_{f}^{-1}(U))=

\displaystyle=\bigcap_{f\in F}\pi(x\rho_{f}^{-1}(U))=\bigcap_{f\in F}\pi(\rho_{f}^{-1}(\rho_{f}(x)U))=\bigcap_{f\in F}(\rho_{K/H})_{f}^{-1}(\pi(\rho_{f}(x)U))\in T_{F}((\rho_{K/H})_{\mathrm{cov}},{\cal U}_{K/H}[U]).

(3) It is enough to verify that, if
B\in T_{F}(\rho_{\mathrm{cov}},{\cal U}_{K}[V])
and
y\in B
, then
B\subseteq yC_{F}(\rho,U)\in{\cal U}_{K}[C_{F}(\rho,U)]
.
Indeed, take
x,y\in B
; for each
f\in F
there exists
x_{f}\in K
such that
B=\bigcap_{f\in F}\rho_{f}^{-1}(x_{f}V)
; hence,

x=\rho_{f}^{-1}(x_{f}w_{f})
and
y=\rho_{f}^{-1}(x_{f}v_{f})
for appropriate
v_{f},w_{f}\in V
. Then,
y^{-1}x=\rho_{f}^{-1}(v_{f}^{-1}w_{f})\in\rho_{f}^{-1}(V^{-1}V)\subseteq\rho_{f}^{-1}(U)
for all
f\in F
, and so
x=y(y^{-1}x)\in yC_{F}(\rho,U)
.

(4) It is enough to show that
K/H\subseteq\bigcup_{d\in D}\pi(dC_{F}(\rho,U))
. Assume for a contradiction that
\pi(k)\notin\bigcup_{d\in D}\pi(dC_{F}(\rho,U))
for some
k\in K
, so that
k\not\in D
, and we verify that
D\cup\{k\}
satisfies
(*)
.
In fact, if
\pi(dt)=\pi(ks)\in\pi(dC_{F}(\rho,V))\cap\pi(kC_{F}(\rho,V))
for some
t,s\in C_{F}(\rho,V)
and
d\in D
, then there exists
h\in H
such that
ks=dth
. Let
s^{\prime}=hs^{-1}h^{-1}\in C_{F}(\rho,V)
(by using that
V=V^{-1}
is invariant), then
k=kss^{-1}=dths^{-1}=dts^{\prime}h
, with
ts^{\prime}\in C_{F}(\rho,V)C_{F}(\rho,V)\subseteq C_{F}(\rho,VV)\subseteq C_{F}(\rho,U)
. Hence,
\pi(k)\in\pi(dC_{F}(\rho,U))
, which contradicts our assumption that
\pi(k)\notin\bigcup_{d\in D}\pi(dC_{F}(\rho,U))
. Therefore,
D\cup\{k\}
satisfies
(*)
, against our assumption that
D
is maximal for
(*)
.
∎

4.3
Proof of the Topological Addition Theorem

The following proposition follows by the results of
[
6
]
, that extend to the setting of cancellative and right amenable monoids the formalism of quasi-tilings of amenable groups introduced by Ornstein and Weiss in
[
47
]
. The reason to refer to
[
6
]
for a statement about amenable groups is that it is a very accessible and detailed source for such a technical argument like quasi-tilings. Moreover, contrary to what we need in this section, many standard sources for amenable group theory in the literature are restricted to the countable case.

Proposition 4.8
.

Let
G
be an amenable group and
{\mathfrak{s}}=\{F_{i}\}_{i\in I}
a right Følner net for
G
. Then, for each
\varepsilon\in(0,1/2)
and
\bar{\jmath}\in I
, there exist
n\in{\mathbb{N}}_{+}
,
\bar{\jmath}<i_{1}<\ldots<i_{n}
in
I
and
\bar{\imath}\in I
such that the family
\mathcal{T}=\{F_{i_{1}},\dots,F_{i_{n}}\}\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{fin}}(G)

\varepsilon
-quasi-tiles
F_{i}
, for all
i\geq\bar{\imath}
in
I
, that is, there is a family
\{C_{1},\dots,C_{n}\}\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{fin}}(G)
such that:

(QT.1)

C_{t}F_{i_{t}}\subseteq F_{i}
and
|C_{t}F_{i_{t}}|\geq(1-\varepsilon)|C_{t}||F_{i_{t}}|
, for all
t=1,\dots,n
;

(QT.2)

the family
\{C_{t}F_{i_{t}}:t=1,\dots,n\}
is pairwise disjoint;

(QT.3)

|F_{i}\setminus\bigcup_{t=1}^{n}C_{t}F_{i_{t}}|\leq\varepsilon|F_{i}|
.

The elements
F_{i_{t}}
of
\mathcal{T}
are called
shapes
or
tiles
, while the
C_{j}
are called
(\mathcal{T},\varepsilon)
-tiling centers
for
F_{i}
.

We apply the above proposition in the proof of the next one.

Proposition 4.9
.

(\rho_{H})_{\mathrm{cov}}\oplus(\rho_{K/H})_{\mathrm{cov}}\colon G\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 6.0pt$$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowright\ $}}\mathrm{cov}(H)\oplus\mathrm{cov}(K/H)
weakly asymptotically dominates
G\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 6.0pt$\rho_{\mathrm{cov}}$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowright\ $}}\mathrm{cov}(K)
.

Proof.

Fix
\varepsilon\in(0,1/2)
and let
{\cal U}\in\mathrm{cov}(K)
. Up to taking a refinement, we can suppose that
{\cal U}
is even (see Lemma
4.7
(1)).
Let
{\mathfrak{s}}=\{F_{i}\}_{i\in I}
be a right Følner net for
G
. By Proposition
4.2
(3), for every
i\in I
,

\log N({\cal U}_{F_{i}})\leq\log N_{K/H}({\cal W}_{F_{i}})+\log N({\cal U}_{F_{i}}|{\cal W}_{F_{i}}).

(4.4)

Next we consider
\log N({\cal U}_{F_{i}}|{\cal W}_{F_{i}})
. Observe that:

•

by Lemma
4.4
, there exists
\bar{\jmath}_{0}\in I
such that (
4.2
) holds with
\bar{\imath}=\bar{\jmath}_{0}
;

•

by Proposition
4.8
, there are
\bar{\jmath}_{0}<i_{1}<\dots<i_{n}
in
I
and
\bar{\jmath}_{1}\in I
such that for all
i\geq\bar{\jmath}_{1}
in
I
,
\mathcal{T}=\{F_{i_{1}},\dots,F_{i_{n}}\}

\varepsilon
-quasi-tiles
F_{i}
;

•

by Proposition
4.2
(6), we can choose
{\cal W}\in\mathrm{cov}(Q)
such that
N({\cal U}_{F_{i_{t}}}|{\cal W})=N({\cal U}_{F_{i_{t}}}|\pi)
for all
t=1,\dots,n
;

•

by Lemma
4.5
, for any
i\in I
,
N({\cal U}_{F_{i}}|\pi)=N_{H}({\cal U}_{F_{i}})=N_{H}({\cal V}_{\rho_{H},F_{i}})=N_{H}({\cal V}_{F_{i}})
, with
{\cal V}=\{U\cap H:U\in{\cal U}\}\in\mathrm{cov}(H)
, as
{\cal U}_{F_{i_{t}}}\cap H=({\cal U}\cap H)_{\rho_{H},F_{i}}=({\cal U}\cap H)_{F_{i}}
since
\rho_{g}^{-1}(H)=H
for every
g\in G
.

Choose
\bar{\jmath}\in I
such that
\bar{\jmath}\geq\bar{\jmath}_{k}
, with
k=0,1
. Fix
i\geq\bar{\jmath}
in
I
, and choose a family
\{C_{1},\dots,C_{n}\}\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{fin}}(G)
of
(\mathcal{T},\varepsilon)
-tiling centers for
F_{i}
. This gives some basic partitions:

F_{i}=F_{i}^{*}\sqcup F_{i}^{\#},\mbox{ with }\ F_{i}^{*}=\bigcup_{t=1}^{n}C_{t}F_{i_{t}}=\bigsqcup_{t=1}^{n}C_{t}F_{i_{t}},\ F_{i}^{\#}=F_{i}\setminus F_{i}^{*}\ \mbox{ and }\ |F_{i}^{\#}|\leq\varepsilon|F_{i}|.

(4.5)

Apply Corollary
4.3
(2) and (3) to get the following inequalities, where the last one makes use also of the last inequality in (
4.5
):

\log N({\cal U}_{F_{i}}|{\cal W}_{F_{i}})\leq\log N({\cal U}_{F_{i}^{*}}|{\cal W}_{F_{i}})+\log N({\cal U}_{F_{i}^{\#}}|{\cal W}_{F_{i}})\leq\log N({\cal U}_{F_{i}^{*}}|{\cal W}_{F_{i}})+\varepsilon|F_{i}|\log N({\cal U}).

(4.6)

To better understand the summand
\log N({\cal U}_{{F_{i}^{*}}}|{\cal W}_{F_{i}})\leq\sum_{t=1}^{n}\log N({\cal U}_{C_{t}F_{i_{t}}}|{\cal W}_{F_{i}})

(see Corollary
4.3
(3) and (
4.5
)), we can study each
N({\cal U}_{C_{t}F_{i_{t}}}|{\cal W}_{F_{i}})
separately, for
t=1,\dots,n
. In particular, by Corollary
4.3
(1) and (3) and Proposition
4.2
(2),

\displaystyle\log N({\cal U}_{C_{t}F_{i_{t}}}|{\cal W}_{F_{i}})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{c\in C_{t}}\log N({\cal U}_{cF_{i_{t}}}|{\cal W}_{F_{i}})=\sum_{c\in C_{t}}\log N({\cal U}_{F_{i_{t}}}|{\cal W}_{c^{-1}F_{i}})\leq|C_{t}|\log N({\cal U}_{F_{i_{t}}}|{\cal W})=|C_{t}|\log N({\cal U}_{F_{i_{t}}}|\pi),

where the last equality holds by the choice of
{\cal W}
. Hence, using (
4.2
) with
\bar{\imath}=\bar{\jmath}
,

\displaystyle\log N({\cal U}_{C_{t}F_{i_{t}}}|{\cal W}_{F_{i}})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{|F_{i_{t}}||C_{t}|}{|F_{i}|}\log N({\cal U}_{F_{i}}|\pi)+{\varepsilon|F_{i_{t}}||C_{t}|}.

Taking the sum over
t
of these last inequalities, we finally get the estimate we were missing in (
4.6
):

\begin{split}\log N({\cal U}_{{F_{i}^{*}}}|{\cal W}_{F_{i}})&\leq\sum_{t=1}^{n}\left(\frac{|C_{t}||F_{i_{t}}|}{|F_{i}|}\log N({\cal U}_{F_{i}}|\pi)+{\varepsilon|C_{t}||F_{i_{t}}|}\right)\leq\\
&\leq\left(\sum_{t=1}^{n}\frac{|C_{t}||F_{i_{t}}|}{|F_{i}|}\right)\log N({\cal U}_{F_{i}}|\pi)+{\varepsilon\sum_{t=1}^{n}|C_{t}||F_{i_{t}}|}\leq\frac{1}{1-\varepsilon}\log N({\cal U}_{F_{i}}|\pi)+{\frac{\varepsilon|F_{i}|}{1-\varepsilon}}.\end{split}

Combining this inequality with (
4.6
), and recalling that
N({\cal U}_{F_{i}}|\pi)=N_{H}({\cal V}_{F_{i}})
,

\log N({\cal U}_{F_{i}}|{\cal W}_{F_{i}})\leq\frac{1}{1-\varepsilon}\log N_{H}({\cal V}_{F_{i}})+\frac{\varepsilon|F_{i}|}{1-\varepsilon}+\varepsilon|F_{i}|\log N({\cal U}).

So, using also (
4.4
), we get that

\displaystyle\log N({\cal U}_{F_{i}})

\displaystyle\leq\log N_{K/H}({\cal W}_{F_{i}})+\frac{1}{1-\varepsilon}\log N_{H}({\cal V}_{F_{i}})+\frac{\varepsilon|F_{i}|}{1-\varepsilon}+\varepsilon|F_{i}|\log N({\cal U})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{1-\varepsilon}(\log N_{K/H}({\cal W}_{F_{i}})+\log N_{H}({\cal V}_{F_{i}}))+\frac{\varepsilon|F_{i}|}{1-\varepsilon}+\varepsilon|F_{i}|\log N({\cal U}).

Dividing by
|F_{i}|
, and considering the last inequality with
\varepsilon=\frac{1}{n}
, we get

\frac{\log N({\cal U}_{F_{i}})}{|F_{i}|}\leq\frac{n}{n-1}\left(\frac{\log N_{H}({\cal V}_{F_{i}})}{|F_{i}|}+\frac{\log N_{K/H}({\cal W}_{F_{i}})}{|F_{i}|}\right)+\frac{1}{n-1}+\frac{1}{n}\log N({\cal U}).

This means that, with
f_{n}(r)=\frac{n}{n-1}r+\frac{1}{n-1}+\frac{1}{n}\log N({\cal U})
, for
r\in{\mathbb{R}}
, and the constant sequence
({\cal V},{\cal W})\in\mathrm{cov}(H)\oplus\mathrm{cov}(K/H)
, the inequality

\frac{v_{\mathrm{cov}}(T_{F_{i}}(\rho_{\mathrm{cov}},{\cal U}))}{|F_{i}|}\leq f_{n}\left(\frac{v_{\mathrm{cov}}(T_{F_{i}}((\rho_{H})_{\mathrm{cov}}\oplus(\rho_{K/H})_{\mathrm{cov}},({\cal V},{\cal W})))}{|F_{i}|}\right),

witnesses the weak asymptotic domination, since
f_{n}(r)-r
uniformly converges to 0 on every bounded interval
[0,C]
.
∎

Proposition 4.10
.

G\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 6.0pt$\rho_{\mathrm{cov}}$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowright\ $}}\mathrm{cov}(K)
dominates
(\rho_{H})_{\mathrm{cov}}\oplus(\rho_{K/H})_{\mathrm{cov}}\colon G\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 6.0pt$$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowright\ $}}\mathrm{cov}(H)\oplus\mathrm{cov}(K/H)
.

Proof.

Given
{\cal U}\in\mathrm{cov}(K)
and
{\cal V}\in\mathrm{cov}(H)
, by Lemma
4.7
(1), there is an open neighborhood
U
of
1\in K
such that
{\cal V}\preceq{\cal U}_{H}[U\cap H]
and
\pi({\cal U})\preceq{\cal U}_{K/H}[U]
. Choose a symmetric and invariant neighborhood
V
of
1\in K
such that, letting
W=VVVV
, we have
WW\subseteq U
. Then
W
is invariant in
K
, so
W\cap H
is invariant in
H
. Given
F\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{fin}}(G)
, select
D\subseteq H
and
E\subseteq K
to be maximal with respect to the following properties:

(
*_{H}
)

d_{1}C_{F}(\rho_{H},W\cap H)\cap d_{2}C_{F}(\rho_{H},W\cap H)=\emptyset
, for all
d_{1}\neq d_{2}
in
D
;

(
*_{K/H}
)

\pi(e_{1}C_{F}(\rho,W))\cap\pi(e_{2}C_{F}(\rho,W))=\emptyset
, for all
e_{1}\neq e_{2}
in
E
.

The existence of such a subset
D
of the compact group
H
follows from the fact that its open subset
A=C_{F}(\rho_{H},W\cap H)\neq\emptyset
has a positive Haar measure, say
a>0
, while
H
has Haar measure 1. Therefore, a set
D
with (
*_{H}
) must have size at most
1/a
, so there exists a maximal one with (
*_{H}
). As far as the existence of
E
is concerned, consider the open subset
B=\pi(C_{F}(\rho_{K/H},W)\neq\emptyset
of
K/H
. If
E\subseteq K
satisfies (
*_{K/H}
), then
e_{1}\pi^{-1}(B)\cap e_{2}\pi^{-1}(B)=\emptyset
, for all
e_{1}\neq e_{2}
in
E
. So by the previous argument, applied this time to the compact group
K
and its open subset
\pi^{-1}(B)
, we conclude that there is a finite upper bound for the cardinalities of
E
satisfying (
*_{K/H}
). In particular, there is a maximal one.

By Lemma
4.7
(2) and (4), we deduce that:

N(T_{F}((\rho_{H})_{\mathrm{cov}},{\cal V}))\leq N(T_{F}((\rho_{H})_{\mathrm{cov}},{\cal U}_{H}[U\cap H]))\leq N({\cal U}_{H}[C_{F}(\rho_{H},U\cap H)])\leq|D|.

Similarly, by Lemma
4.7
(2) and (4), we obtain that:

N(T_{F}((\rho_{K/H})_{\mathrm{cov}},\pi({\cal U})))\leq N(T_{F}((\rho_{K/H})_{\mathrm{cov}},{\cal U}_{K/H}[U])\leq N({\cal U}_{K/H}[C_{F}(\rho,U)])\leq|E|.

In particular,

N(T_{F}((\rho_{H})_{\mathrm{cov}}\oplus(\rho_{K/H})_{\mathrm{cov}},({\cal V},\pi({\cal U})))=N(T_{F}((\rho_{H})_{\mathrm{cov}},{\cal V}))\cdot N(T_{F}((\rho_{K/H})_{\mathrm{cov}},\pi({\cal U})))\leq|D|\cdot|E|.

Moreover, the following property holds:

(
*
)

(e_{1}C_{F}(\rho,VV)d_{1})\cap(e_{2}C_{F}(\rho,VV)d_{2})=\emptyset
, for all
(e_{1},d_{1})\neq(e_{2},d_{2})
in
E\times D
.

In fact, assume that
e_{1}c_{1}d_{1}=e_{2}c_{2}d_{2}
for some
c_{1},c_{2}\in C_{F}(\rho,VV)
,
e_{1},e_{2}\in E
, and
d_{1},d_{2}\in D
. Then,

\pi(e_{1}c_{1})=\pi(e_{1}c_{1}d_{1})=\pi(e_{2}c_{2}d_{2})=\pi(e_{2}c_{2})\in\pi(e_{1}C_{F}(\rho,VV))\cap\pi(e_{2}C_{F}(\rho,VV))\subseteq\pi(e_{1}C_{F}(\rho,W))\cap\pi(e_{2}C_{F}(\rho,W)),

and so
e_{1}=e_{2}
by (
*_{K/H}
). Hence,
c_{1}d_{1}=c_{2}d_{2}
and, therefore,

c=c_{1}^{-1}c_{2}=d_{1}d_{2}^{-1}\in H\cap C_{F}(\rho,W)=C_{F}(\rho_{H},W\cap H),

showing that
d_{1}=cd_{2}\in C_{F}(\rho_{H},W\cap H)d_{2}=d_{2}C_{F}(\rho_{H},W\cap H)
, as
C_{F}(\rho_{H},W\cap H)
is invariant in
H

(being
W\cap H
invariant in the
S
-invariant subgroup
H
). This gives
d_{1}=d_{2}
by (
*_{H}
). Hence,
\bigcup_{(e,d)\in E\times D}eC_{F}(\rho,VV)d\subseteq K
is a disjoint union of
|E|\cdot|D|
-many measurable subsets, each of which has the same measure of
C_{F}(\rho,VV)
(use that, being
K
compact, it is unimodular and, therefore,
\mu
is both left and right invariant), and so

|E|\cdot|D|\cdot\mu(C_{F}(\rho,VV))\leq\mu(K)\leq N({\cal U}_{K}[C_{F}(\rho,VV)])\cdot\mu(C_{F}(\rho,VV)).

Hence,
|E|\cdot|D|\leq N({\cal U}_{K}[C_{F}(\rho,VV)])
. By Lemma
4.7
(3),
N({\cal U}_{K}[C_{F}(\rho,VV)])\leq N(T_{F}(\rho_{\mathrm{cov}},{\cal U}_{K}[V]))
and, combining all these estimates, we get:

N(T_{F}((\rho_{H})_{\mathrm{cov}}\oplus(\rho_{K/H})_{\mathrm{cov}},({\cal V},\pi({\cal U})))\leq|D|\cdot|E|\leq N(T_{F}(\rho_{\mathrm{cov}},{\cal U}_{K}[V]).

Taking logarithms gives

v_{\mathrm{cov}}(T_{F}((\rho_{H})_{\mathrm{cov}}\oplus(\rho_{K/H})_{\mathrm{cov}},({\cal V},\pi({\cal U}))))\leq v_{\mathrm{cov}}(T_{F}(\rho_{\mathrm{cov}},{\cal U}_{K}[V]))
.∎

As a consequence of the above propositions and of the results of §
3.3
, we obtain the proof of (
at
top
):

Proof of the Topological Addition Theorem.

First assume that
K\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 7.0pt$\rho$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowleft\ $}}G
is a right action of an amenable group
G
on a compact group
K
and that
H
is an
S
-invariant closed subgroup of
K
. Fix a right Følner net
{\mathfrak{s}}
for
G
. Since
\rho_{\mathrm{cov}}
and
(\rho_{H})_{\mathrm{cov}}\oplus(\rho_{K/H})_{\mathrm{cov}}
are weakly asymptotically equivalent by Propositions
4.9
and
4.10
,

h(\rho_{\mathrm{cov}},{\mathfrak{s}})=h((\rho_{H})_{\mathrm{cov}}\oplus(\rho_{K/H})_{\mathrm{cov}},{\mathfrak{s}})=h((\rho_{H})_{\mathrm{cov}},{\mathfrak{s}})+h({(\rho_{K/H})_{\mathrm{cov}}},{\mathfrak{s}}),

(4.7)

by Propositions
1.16
and
1.15
. Hence, (
4.7
) gives
h_{\mathrm{top}}(\rho)=h_{\mathrm{top}}(\rho_{{H}})+h_{\mathrm{top}}(\rho_{K/H})
as already now that, by Example
1.23
,
h_{\mathrm{top}}(\rho)=h(\rho_{\mathrm{cov}},{\mathfrak{s}})
,
h_{\mathrm{top}}(\rho_{H})=h((\rho_{H})_{\mathrm{cov}},{\mathfrak{s}})
and
h_{\mathrm{top}}(\rho_{K/H})=h({(\rho_{K/H})_{\mathrm{cov}}},{\mathfrak{s}})
.

Now consider the general case of a right
S
-action
K\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 7.0pt$\rho$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowleft\ $}}S
of a cancellative and right amenable monoid
S
on a compact group
K
and let
H
be a closed
S
-invariant (not necessarily normal) subgroup of
K
. As usual, let
G=S^{-1}S
be the group of left fractions of
S
.
By Corollary
3.14
(1), we can identify
H^{*}
with a closed
S
-invariant subgroup of
K^{*}
(so that it makes sense to consider the space of left
H^{*}
-cosets
K^{*}/H^{*}
), and by Corollary
3.14
(2), we can identify
K^{*}/H^{*}
with
(K/H)^{*}
. This allows us to apply the previous case and write

h_{\mathrm{top}}(\rho^{*})=h_{\mathrm{top}}((\rho^{*})_{H^{*}})+h_{\mathrm{top}}((\rho^{*})_{K^{*}/H^{*}}).

(4.8)

In view of the above identifications,

h_{\mathrm{top}}((\rho^{*})_{H^{*}})=h_{\mathrm{top}}((\rho_{H})^{*})\quad\text{and}\quad h_{\mathrm{top}}((\rho^{*})_{K^{*}/H^{*}})=h_{\mathrm{top}}((\rho_{K/H})^{*}).

Furthermore, by Theorem
3.15
, we deduce that
h_{\mathrm{top}}(\rho^{*})=h_{\mathrm{top}}(\rho)
,
h_{\mathrm{top}}((\rho_{H})^{*})=h_{\mathrm{top}}(\rho_{H})
and
h_{\mathrm{top}}((\rho_{K/H})^{*})=h_{\mathrm{top}}(\rho_{K/H})
. Making these substitutions in (
4.8
), we get

h_{\mathrm{top}}(\rho)=h_{\mathrm{top}}(\rho_{H})+h_{\mathrm{top}}(\rho_{K/H}).\qed

Question 4.11
.

Is it possible to prove the general version of AT
top
without making recurse to the Ore colocalization?

5
Proof of the Bridge Theorem and the Algebraic Addition Theorem

The aim of this last section, is to prove the Bridge Theorem, as announced in the introduction. We start with a lemma showing how, for a cancellative and right amenable monoid
S
, the kernel
\mathrm{Ker}(\lambda)
of a left
S
-action
S\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 6.0pt$\lambda$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowright\ $}}X
on a discrete Abelian group (from §
2.1
) and the surjective core
E(\rho)
of a right
S
-action
K\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 7.0pt$\rho$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowleft\ $}}S
on a compact Abelian group (from §
3.2
) are both the dual construction of each other. Recall that
\bar{X}=X/\mathrm{Ker}(\lambda)
and
\bar{K}=E(\rho)
, and that
S\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 6.0pt$\bar{\lambda}$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowright\ $}}\bar{X}
and
\bar{K}\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 7.0pt$\bar{\rho}$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowleft\ $}}S
are the induced
S
-actions by
\lambda
and
\rho
, respectively.

Lemma 5.1
.

The following statements hold true:

(1)

Given a left
S
-action
S\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 6.0pt$\lambda$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowright\ $}}X
on a discrete Abelian group
X
, let
K={X}^{\wedge}
and
K\hskip 5.0pt\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{6.02773pt}{\hskip-2.5pt$\rho={\lambda}^{\wedge}$}}}{\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{$\ \curvearrowleft\ $}}\hskip 6.0ptS
. Then,
\mathrm{Ker}(\lambda)^{\perp}=E(\rho)
and the dual of
S\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 6.0pt$\bar{\lambda}$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowright\ $}}\bar{X}
is conjugated to
\bar{K}\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 7.0pt$\bar{\rho}$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowleft\ $}}S
.

(2)

Given a right
S
-action
K\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 7.0pt$\rho$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowleft\ $}}S
on a compact Abelian group
K
, let
X={K}^{\wedge}
and
S\hskip 5.0pt\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{6.02773pt}{\hskip-3.0pt$\lambda={\rho}^{\wedge}$}}}{\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{$\ \curvearrowright\ $}}\hskip 6.0ptX
. Then,
E(\rho)^{\perp}=\mathrm{Ker}(\lambda)
and the dual of
\bar{K}\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 7.0pt$\bar{\rho}$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowleft\ $}}S
is conjugated to
S\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 6.0pt$\bar{\lambda}$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowright\ $}}\bar{X}
.

Proof.

Let us just prove (1), as part (2) follows similarly. Consider
(S,\leq)
preordered by its right divisibility relation, then
\mathrm{Ker}(\lambda)
and
E(\rho)
are, respectively, the supremum in
\mathcal{L}(X)
of the directed family
\{\mathrm{Ker}(\lambda_{s}):s\in S\}
and the infimum in
\mathcal{L}(K)
of the downward directed family
\{\rho_{s}(K):s\in S\}
. By the properties of the lattice anti-isomorphism
(-)^{\perp}\colon\mathcal{L}(X)\to\mathcal{L}(K)
, one deduces immediately that
\mathrm{Ker}(\lambda)^{\perp}=E(\rho)
and, therefore,

{\bar{X}}^{\wedge}={(X/\mathrm{Ker}(\lambda))}^{\wedge}\cong\mathrm{Ker}(\lambda)^{\perp}=E(\rho)=\bar{K}.

(5.1)

Finally, for each
s\in S
, we have that
\lambda_{s}(\mathrm{Ker}(\lambda))=\mathrm{Ker}(\lambda)
and
\bar{\lambda}_{s}
is the injective endomorphism induced by
\lambda_{s}
on the quotient
\bar{X}=X/\mathrm{Ker}(\lambda)
. As the Pontryagin duality sends (endomorphisms of) quotient groups to (endomorphisms of) closed subgroups of the dual,
{{\bar{\lambda}}_{s}}^{\wedge}
can be identified with the restriction
\bar{\rho}_{s}
of
\rho_{s}={\lambda_{s}}^{\wedge}
to
\bar{K}
. In other words, the isomorphism in (
5.1
) is
S
-equivariant.
∎

In the following lemma we show that “the Ore colocalization of a right action on a compact Abelian group is the dual of the Ore localization of its dual action” and, vice versa, that “the Ore localization of a left action on a discrete Abelian group is the dual of the Ore colocalization of its dual action” (see §
2.2
and §
3.3
for the definitions of the Ore localization
G\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 6.0pt$\lambda^{*}$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowright\ $}}X^{*}
and the Ore colocalization
K^{*}\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 7.0pt$\rho^{*}$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowleft\ $}}G
, respectively).

Lemma 5.2
.

The following statements hold true:

(1)

Given a left
S
-action
S\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 6.0pt$\lambda$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowright\ $}}X
on a discrete Abelian group
X
, let
K={X}^{\wedge}
and
K\hskip 5.0pt\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{6.02773pt}{\hskip-2.5pt$\rho={\lambda}^{\wedge}$}}}{\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{$\ \curvearrowleft\ $}}\hskip 6.0ptS
.
Then the dual of
G\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 6.0pt$\lambda^{*}$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowright\ $}}X^{*}
is conjugated to
K^{*}\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 7.0pt$\rho^{*}$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowleft\ $}}G
.

(2)

Given a right
S
-action
K\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 7.0pt$\rho$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowleft\ $}}S
on a compact Abelian group
K
, let
X={K}^{\wedge}
and
S\hskip 5.0pt\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{6.02773pt}{\hskip-3.0pt$\lambda={\rho}^{\wedge}$}}}{\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{$\ \curvearrowright\ $}}\hskip 6.0ptX
.
Then the dual of
K^{*}\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 7.0pt$\rho^{*}$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowleft\ $}}G
is conjugated to
G\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 6.0pt$\lambda^{*}$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowright\ $}}X^{*}
.

Proof.

We just prove part (1), as (2) follows similarly. In Definition
2.5
we have introduced
X^{*}
as a direct limit of a direct system
\mathfrak{X}
of copies of
\bar{X}
, indexed by
(G,\leq_{S})
, and with transition maps given by suitable maps of the form
\bar{\lambda}_{s}
for some
s\in S
. Dually, in Definition
3.13
, we have introduced
K^{*}
as the inverse limit of an inverse system
\mathfrak{K}
of copies of
\bar{K}
, indexed by
(G,\leq_{S})
, and with transition maps given by suitable maps of the form
\bar{\rho}_{s}
for some
s\in S
. Now, since Lemma
5.1
(1) allows us to identify
{\bar{X}}^{\wedge}\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 7.0pt${\bar{\lambda}}^{\wedge}$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowleft\ $}}S
with
\bar{K}\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 7.0pt$\bar{\rho}$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowleft\ $}}S
, we deduce that the two inverse systems
{\mathfrak{X}}^{\wedge}
and
\mathfrak{K}
are isomorphic and, therefore, so are their limits:
{(X^{*})}^{\wedge}\cong K^{*}
. Furthermore, this isomorphism is
S
-equivariant since the commutative diagrams of Lemma
2.6
(2) are sent, via Pontryagin duality, to the commutative diagrams that appear in Lemma
3.12
(2). In fact,
\rho^{*}
is, by definition, the unique right
S
-action that makes these diagrams commute; as
{(\lambda^{*})}^{\wedge}
makes the same diagrams commute, the two actions have to be conjugated.
∎

Proof of the Bridge Theorem.

(1) Let
K\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 7.0pt$\rho$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowleft\ $}}S
be a right
S
-action on a compact Abelian group
K
and
S\hskip 5.0pt\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{6.02773pt}{\hskip-3.0pt$\lambda={\rho}^{\wedge}$}}}{\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{$\ \curvearrowright\ $}}\hskip 6.0ptX={K}^{\wedge}
.
With the notation of §
2
and §
3
, we have proved in Theorems
2.9
and
3.15
, respectively, that

h_{\mathrm{top}}(\rho)=h_{\mathrm{top}}(\rho^{*})\quad\text{and}\quad h_{\mathrm{alg}}(\lambda)=h_{\mathrm{alg}}(\lambda^{*}).

Furthermore,
\rho^{*}
is conjugated to
{(\lambda^{*})}^{\wedge}
by Lemma
5.2
(1) and so
h_{\mathrm{top}}(\rho^{*})=h_{\mathrm{top}}({(\lambda^{*})}^{\wedge})
by Proposition
1.21
(1), while
h_{\mathrm{top}}({(\lambda^{*})}^{\wedge})=h_{\mathrm{alg}}(\lambda^{*})
by Theorem
A.24
. From the above equalities we conclude that

h_{\mathrm{top}}(\rho)=h_{\mathrm{top}}(\rho^{*})=h_{\mathrm{top}}({(\lambda^{*})}^{\wedge})=h_{\mathrm{alg}}(\lambda^{*})=h_{\mathrm{alg}}(\lambda).

(2) Let
S\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 6.0pt$\lambda$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowright\ $}}X
be a left
S
-action on a discrete Abelian group
X
and
K={X}^{\wedge}\hskip 5.0pt\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{6.02773pt}{\hskip-2.5pt$\rho={\lambda}^{\wedge}$}}}{\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{$\ \curvearrowleft\ $}}\hskip 6.0ptS
.
Since the left
S
-action
{\rho}^{\wedge}={\lambda}^{\wedge\wedge}
is conjugated to
\lambda
(as noted in the introduction), we deduce that
h_{\mathrm{alg}}(\lambda)=h_{\mathrm{alg}}({\lambda}^{\wedge\wedge})
by Proposition
1.21
(1). By part (1),
h_{\mathrm{top}}(\rho)=h_{\mathrm{alg}}({\rho}^{\wedge})=h_{\mathrm{alg}}(\lambda)
, as required.
∎

The above proof shows in particular that items (1) and (2) in the Bridge Theorem are equivalent. This makes it natural to state the Bridge Theorem asymmetrically, that is, in our case from the point of view of
K\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 7.0pt$\rho$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowleft\ $}}S
.

Proof of the Algebraic Addition Theorem.

Given a left
S
-action
S\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 6.0pt$\lambda$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowright\ $}}X
on a discrete Abelian group
X
and letting
Y
be an
S
-invariant
subgroup of
X
, denote by
\lambda_{Y}
and
\lambda_{X/Y}
the obvious left
S
-actions induced by
\lambda
on
Y
and
X/Y
, respectively. Furthermore, consider the compact Abelian group
K={X}^{\wedge}
, its closed subgroup
H=Y^{\perp}
and its quotient group
K/H
, and the right
S
-action
\rho={\lambda}^{\wedge}
. Then
H
is
S
-invariant and the action
\rho_{H}
induced by
\rho
on
H
by restriction is conjugated to
{(\lambda_{X/Y})}^{\wedge}
, while the right
S
-action
\rho_{K/H}
induced by
\rho
on
K/H
is conjugated to
{(\lambda_{Y})}^{\wedge}
. We can now conclude via the following series of equalities:

\displaystyle h_{\mathrm{alg}}(\lambda)

\displaystyle=h_{\mathrm{top}}(\rho)

by the Bridge Theorem;

\displaystyle=h_{\mathrm{top}}(\rho_{H})+h_{\mathrm{top}}(\rho_{K/H})

by the Topological Addition Theorem;

\displaystyle=h_{\mathrm{top}}({(\lambda_{X/Y})}^{\wedge}\,)+h_{\mathrm{top}}({{(\lambda_{Y})}^{\wedge}})

by Proposition
1.27
(1);

\displaystyle=h_{\mathrm{alg}}(\lambda_{X/Y})+h_{\mathrm{alg}}(\lambda_{Y})

\displaystyle\text{by the Bridge Theorem}.

∎

Question 5.3
.

Is it possible to give a direct proof of
(\text{\sc at}_{\mathrm{alg}})
instead of deducing it from
(\text{\sc at}_{\mathrm{top}})
through the Bridge Theorem?

Appendix A
Bridge Theorem for the entropies of amenable group actions

In this appendix, we consider a discrete Abelian group
X
, its dual compact Abelian group
K={X}^{\wedge}
(identifying
X
with
{K}^{\wedge}
) and the following actions of an infinite amenable group
G
:
G\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 6.0pt$\lambda$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowright\ $}}X
and
K\hskip 5.0pt\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{6.02773pt}{\hskip-2.5pt$\rho={\lambda}^{\wedge}$}}}{\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{$\ \curvearrowleft\ $}}\hskip 6.0ptG
.
Our goal is to prove the Bridge Theorem, that is, that
h_{\mathrm{alg}}(\lambda)=h_{\mathrm{top}}(\rho)
.

Remark A.1
.

When
X
is torsion (i.e.,
\mathcal{L}^{\mathrm{fin}}(X)
is cofinal in
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{fin}}^{0}(X)
– see Example
1.6
(3)) and so equivalently
K
is profinite (i.e.,
\mathcal{L}^{\mathrm{o}}(K)
is cofinal in
\mathrm{cov}(K)
– see Example
1.8
(6)),
we have seen in Examples
1.19
(4) and
1.24
(4) that the algebraic and the topological entropies may be written, respectively, only in terms of
\mathcal{L}^{\mathrm{fin}}(X)
and
\mathcal{L}^{\mathrm{o}}(K)
, namely, for a right Følner net
{\mathfrak{s}}
for
G
,
h_{\mathrm{alg}}(\lambda)=h((\lambda_{\mathcal{P}})_{\restriction\mathcal{L}^{\mathrm{fin}}(X)},{\mathfrak{s}})
and
h_{\mathrm{top}}(\rho)=h((\rho_{\mathrm{cov}})_{\restriction\mathcal{L}^{\mathrm{o}}(K)},{\mathfrak{s}})
.
By using the isomorphism of normed monoids
\mathcal{L}^{\mathrm{fin}}(X)\cong\mathcal{L}^{\mathrm{o}}(K)
given by
F\mapsto F^{\perp}
from Example
1.8
(4), one can see that
(\lambda_{\mathcal{P}})_{\restriction\mathcal{L}^{\mathrm{fin}}(X)}
and
(\rho_{\mathrm{cov}})_{\restriction\mathcal{L}^{\mathrm{o}}(K)}
are conjugated and so conclude that
h_{\mathrm{alg}}(\lambda)=h_{\mathrm{top}}(\rho)
by Proposition
1.16
.
This is done in
[
15
,
24
,
63
]
.

Unfortunately, in general this is not enough (think for example of the case when
X
is torsion-free, where
\mathcal{L}^{\mathrm{fin}}(X)=\{0\}
and
\mathcal{L}^{\mathrm{o}}(K)=\{K\}
) but the idea at the base of the above strategy is still valid: to prove the Bridge Theorem one may try to suitably “approximate” the normed monoids
{\mathfrak{F}}(X)
and
\mathrm{cov}(K)
by some other normed monoids: following an idea of
J. Peters
, we consider the monoids
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{alg}}(X)
and
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{top}}(K)
in §
A.2
, and verify that they are isomorphic.

This appendix is organized as follows:

–

in §
A.1
we recall the needed definitions and results from harmonic analysis;

–

in §
A.2
we introduce the algebraic and the topological Peters monoids
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{alg}}(X)
and
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{top}}(K)
, and the canonical left
G
-actions
\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}}
and
\rho_{\mathrm{top}}
on them induced by
\lambda
and
\rho
respectively, and we verify the isomorphism of normed monoids
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{alg}}(X)\cong\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{top}}(K)
that permits to see that
\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}}
and
\rho_{\mathrm{top}}
are conjugated;

–

in §
A.3
and §
A.4
we prove that, respectively,
the left
G
-action
\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}}
on
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{alg}}(X)
and the left
G
-action
\lambda_{\mathfrak{F}}
on
{\mathfrak{F}}(X)

are asymptotically equivalent, and the same holds true for the left
G
-actions
\rho_{\mathrm{top}}
on
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{top}}(K)
and
\rho_{\mathrm{cov}}
on
\mathrm{cov}(K)
;

–

finally, in §
A.5
, we put all the pieces together to prove the Bridge Theorem.

A.1
Needed facts from harmonic analysis

For the first part of this subsection let
\Gamma
be an infinite
1
1

1

The results in this subsection remain true for finite locally compact Abelian groups (i.e., those that are both compact and discrete). Assuming
\Gamma
to be infinite is just a technical simplification. Furthermore, the entropy (algebraic or topological) of
G
-actions on finite groups is always
0
, so this restriction does not exclude any non-trivial case.
locally compact Abelian group and we fix a Haar measure
\mu
on
\Gamma
. We denote by
{\mathfrak{U}}(\Gamma)
the family of symmetric compact neighborhoods of
0
in
\Gamma
.
We are mainly interested in the case when
\Gamma=X
is discrete, so

\mu
is the counting measure
\mu(Y)=\sup\{|F|:F\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{fin}}(Y)\}
for
Y\subseteq X
and
\mathfrak{U}(X)=\mathfrak{F}(X)
, and when
\Gamma=K
is compact, in which case there is a unique Haar measure such that
\mu(K)=1
.

In this appendix we use the unit circle
\mathbb{S}
in
\mathbb{C}
in place of
{\mathbb{T}}
when we consider the dual group
{\Gamma}^{\wedge}
, so that each
\gamma\in{\Gamma}^{\wedge}
is a continuous complex-valued function
\gamma\colon\Gamma\to\mathbb{S}\subseteq\mathbb{C}
. This motivates us to use the multiplicative notation for the group
{\Gamma}^{\wedge}
, with the sole exception of its neutral element that we write
0_{{\Gamma}^{\wedge}}
(it should be kept in mind that this is the constant function
1
on
\Gamma
).

Given a subset
E\subseteq\Gamma
, we denote by
\mathrm{cl}(E)
its closure in
\Gamma
. For a Borel function
\varphi\colon\Gamma\to\mathbb{C}
, we define its
support
by:
\mathrm{supp}(\varphi)=\mathrm{cl}{\{x\in\Gamma:\varphi(x)\neq 0\}}\subseteq\Gamma
. Furthermore, we say that
\varphi
is:

–

absolutely integrable
if
\|\varphi\|_{1}=\int_{x\in\Gamma}|\varphi(x)|\mathrm{d}\mu(x)<\infty
. We denote by
L^{1}(\Gamma)
the space of absolutely integrable functions, where we have identified those that coincide almost everywhere. By
[
51
, §E7]
,
\|-\|_{1}
induces a norm on
L^{1}(\Gamma)
(so the triangular inequality holds);

–

positive
if it is real-valued (i.e.,
\varphi(\Gamma)\subseteq{\mathbb{R}}
) and
\varphi(x)\geq 0
(in
{\mathbb{R}}
), for all
x\in\Gamma
. Given a set
V
of complex-valued functions on
\Gamma
, we denote by
V^{+}
the subset of all positive functions in
V
;

–

positive-definite
if, for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}_{+}
,
x_{1},\dots,x_{n}\in\Gamma
and
c_{1},\dots,c_{n}\in\mathbb{C}
, the following holds:

\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}c_{i}\overline{c_{j}}\varphi(x_{i}-x_{j})\in{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}
.
We let
\mathfrak{P}(\Gamma)
be the set of continuous and positive-definite functions on
\Gamma
.
As every positive-definite function coincides with a continuous one almost everywhere (see
[
23
, (3.21) and (3.35)]
), it is not restrictive to assume continuity.

Obviously,
{\Gamma}^{\wedge}\subseteq\mathfrak{P}(\Gamma)
.

For the proof of the following properties of positive-definite functions we refer to
[
51
, §1.4.1]
.

Lemma A.2
.

In the above notation, let
\varphi\in\mathfrak{P}(\Gamma)
. Then
\varphi^{\star}
, defined by
\varphi^{\star}(x)=\varphi(-x)
for all
x\in\Gamma
, belongs to
\mathfrak{P}(\Gamma)
and

(1)

\varphi^{\star}(x)=\overline{\varphi(x)}
and
|\varphi(x)|\leq\varphi(0)
, for all
x\in\Gamma
. In particular,
\varphi(0)=\overline{\varphi(0)}\in{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}
;

(2)

if
\varphi\in\mathfrak{P}(\Gamma)^{+}
, then
\varphi=\varphi^{\star}
. In particular,
\varphi(0)
is a maximum for
\varphi
;

(3)

if
\varphi\in\mathfrak{P}(\Gamma)^{+}
has compact support, then
\|\varphi\|_{1}\leq\varphi(0)\cdot\mu(\mathrm{supp}(\varphi))
.

If
\varphi,\psi\in L^{1}(\Gamma)
, then
\int_{y\in\Gamma}|\varphi(y)\psi(x-y)|\mathrm{d}\mu(y)<\infty
for almost all
x\in\Gamma
(see
[
51
, §1.1.6(e)]
), and so the
convolution

\varphi*\psi\colon\Gamma\to\mathbb{C}
is defined almost everywhere by the following formula:

(\varphi*\psi)(x)=\int_{y\in\Gamma}\varphi(y)\psi(x-y)\mathrm{d}\mu(y).

By
[
51
, §1.1.6]
, if
\varphi,\psi\in L^{1}(\Gamma)
, then
\|\varphi*\psi\|_{1}\leq\|\varphi\|_{1}\|\psi\|_{1}<\infty
, so that
\varphi*\psi\in L^{1}(\Gamma)
.

Defining
\varphi_{x}\colon\Gamma\to\mathbb{C}
as
\varphi_{x}(y)=\varphi(xy)
for all
y\in\Gamma
, we get
(\varphi*\psi)(x)=\int_{y\in\Gamma}\varphi(y)\psi_{-y}(x)\mathrm{d}\mu(y)
. For the proof of the following properties of convolutions we refer to
[
51
, §1.1.6, §1.1.7]
:

Lemma A.3
.

In the above notation, let
\varphi,

\psi,

\xi\in L^{1}(\Gamma)
. Then:

(1)

(\varphi*\psi)*\xi=\varphi*(\psi*\xi)
,
\varphi*\psi=\psi*\varphi
and
\xi*(\varphi+\psi)=\xi*\varphi+\xi*\psi
;

(2)

\varphi_{x}*\psi=\varphi*\psi_{x}=(\varphi*\psi)_{x}
, for all
x\in\Gamma
; in particular,

(\varphi_{1}*\ldots*\varphi_{k})_{x}=(\varphi_{1})_{x_{1}}*\ldots*(\varphi_{k})_{x_{k}}
whenever
x=x_{1}+\ldots+x_{k}
, where
\varphi_{1},\ldots,\varphi_{k}\in L^{1}(\Gamma)
and
x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}\in\Gamma
;

(3)

\mathrm{supp}(\varphi*\psi)\subseteq\mathrm{supp}(\varphi)+\mathrm{supp}(\psi)
, so
\varphi*\psi
has compact support, provided
\varphi
and
\psi
have compact support.

For the sake of completeness we include a proof of the following properties needed in the sequel.

Lemma A.4
.

Let
\varphi,

\psi\in L^{1}(\Gamma)^{+}
. Then:

(1)

\|\varphi-\psi\|_{1}\geq\|\varphi\|_{1}-\|\psi\|_{1}
;

(2)

\|\varphi*\psi\|_{1}=\|\varphi\|_{1}\|\psi\|_{1}
;

(3)

\varphi*\varphi^{\star}
is positive-definite, provided
\varphi\in L^{2}(\Gamma)
(that is,
\int_{x\in\Gamma}|\varphi(x)|^{2}\mathrm{d}\mu(x)<\infty
);

(4)

(\varphi*\psi)(x)\leq\varphi(0)\cdot\|\psi\|_{1}
, provided
\varphi\in\mathfrak{P}(\Gamma)
;

(5)

if
\varphi_{1},\ldots,\varphi_{k}\in L^{1}(\Gamma)^{+}
and
\psi_{1},\ldots,\psi_{k}\in L^{1}(\Gamma)^{+}
, with
\varphi_{j}\leq\psi_{j}
for
j=1,\ldots,k
, then
\varphi_{1}*\ldots*\varphi_{k}\leq\psi_{1}*\ldots*\psi_{k}
.

Proof.

(1) For all
x\in\Gamma
, we have
|\varphi(x)-\psi(x)|\geq|\varphi(x)|-|\psi(x)|
. Furthermore, by linearity of integration,

\|\varphi-\psi\|_{1}=\int_{x\in\Gamma}|\varphi(x)-\psi(x)|\mathrm{d}\mu(x)\geq\int_{x\in\Gamma}|\varphi(x)|\mathrm{d}\mu(x)-\int_{x\in\Gamma}|\psi(x)|\mathrm{d}\mu(x)=\|\varphi\|_{1}-\|\psi\|_{1}.

(2) By the proof of
[
51
, Theorem 1.1.6(e)]
we have that
\int_{x\in\Gamma}\left(\int_{y\in\Gamma}|\varphi(x-y)\psi(y)|\mathrm{d}\mu(y)\right)\mathrm{d}\mu(x)=\|\varphi\|_{1}\|\psi\|_{1}
(this is a consequence of the Fubini Theorem). It remains to take into account that, when
\varphi
and
\psi
are positive, then

\displaystyle\int_{x\in\Gamma}\left(\int_{y\in\Gamma}|\varphi(x-y)\psi(y)|\mathrm{d}\mu(y)\right)\mathrm{d}\mu(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{x\in\Gamma}\left(\int_{y\in\Gamma}\varphi(x-y)\psi(y)\mathrm{d}\mu(y)\right)\mathrm{d}\mu(x)=

\displaystyle=\int_{x\in\Gamma}\left|\int_{y\in\Gamma}\varphi(x-y)\psi(y)\mathrm{d}\mu(y)\right|\mathrm{d}\mu(x)=\|\varphi*\psi\|_{1}.

(3) follows by
[
51
, §1.4.2]
and (5) is clear.

(4) By Lemma
A.2
(1), we have
\varphi(\Gamma)\subseteq[0,\varphi(0)]\subseteq{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}
, so by Lemma
A.3
(1),

(\varphi*\psi)(x)=(\psi*\varphi)(x)=\int_{y\in\Gamma}\varphi(x-y)\psi(y)\mathrm{d}\mu(y)\leq\varphi(0)\cdot\int_{y\in\Gamma}\psi(y)\mathrm{d}\mu(y)=\varphi(0)\cdot\|\psi\|_{1}.\qed

As a consequence of the above lemmas, we get:

Corollary A.5
.

For each
U\in{\mathfrak{U}}(\Gamma)
, there is a non-zero
\varphi\in L^{1}(\Gamma)^{+}\cap\mathfrak{P}(\Gamma)
with
\mathrm{supp}(\varphi)\subseteq U
.

Proof.

Choose
V\in{\mathfrak{U}}(\Gamma)
such that
V-V\subseteq U
and an open subset
V
of
\Gamma
with
0\in V^{\prime}\subseteq V
. As
\Gamma
is Tychonoff, there is a continuous function
f\colon\Gamma\to[0,1]
such that
f(0)=1
and
\mathrm{supp}(f)\subseteq\mathrm{cl}(V^{\prime})\subseteq V
, so that
\mathrm{supp}(f^{\star})\subseteq-V
. Letting
\varphi=f*f^{\star}
, we have
\mathrm{supp}(\varphi)\subseteq V-V\subseteq U
and
\varphi\in L^{1}(\Gamma)
by Lemmas
A.3
(3) and
A.4
(2),
\varphi\in\mathfrak{P}(\Gamma)
by Lemma
A.4
(3), and it is easy to see that
\varphi
is non-zero and positive.
∎

For a subset
C
of
\Gamma
we denote by
\chi_{C}\colon\Gamma\to\mathbb{C}
the
characteristic function
of
C
(i.e.,
\chi_{C}(x)=1
if
x\in C
and
\chi_{C}(x)=0
otherwise). If
C
is compact and symmetric, then
\chi_{C}\in L^{1}(\Gamma)^{+}
and
\chi_{C}=(\chi_{C})^{\star}
. By Lemma
A.4
(1) and (4), we get item (1) in the following corollary. Item (2) follows from Lemma
A.4
(2) and
\|\chi_{C}\|_{1}=\mu(C)
.

Corollary A.6
.

Let
C\in{\mathfrak{U}}(\Gamma)
, then:

(1)

\chi_{C}*\chi_{C}\in L^{1}(\Gamma)^{+}\cap\mathfrak{P}(\Gamma)
;

(2)

\|\chi_{C}*\chi_{C}\|_{1}=\mu(C)^{2}
.

A.1.1
The Fourier transform

The
Fourier transform
of
\varphi\in L^{1}(\Gamma)
is defined as

\widehat{\varphi}\colon{\Gamma}^{\wedge}\to\mathbb{C},\quad\text{with }\quad\widehat{\varphi}(\gamma)=(\varphi*\gamma)(0)=\int_{y\in\Gamma}\varphi(y)\gamma(-y)\mathrm{d}\mu(y)=\int_{y\in\Gamma}\varphi(y)\gamma^{-1}(y)\mathrm{d}\mu(y)=\int_{y\in\Gamma}\varphi(y)\overline{\gamma(y)}\mathrm{d}\mu(y).

Then
\widehat{\varphi}
is a bounded continuous function on
{\Gamma}^{\wedge}
that vanishes at infinity (see
[
23
, (4.13)]
). In case
\varphi
is positive-definite, we can say more:

Lemma A.7
.

If
\varphi\in L^{1}(\Gamma)^{+}\cap\mathfrak{P}(\Gamma)
, then
\widehat{\varphi}\in L^{1}({\Gamma}^{\wedge})^{+}\cap\mathfrak{P}({\Gamma}^{\wedge})
.

Proof.

Pick
\varphi\in L^{1}(\Gamma)^{+}\cap\mathfrak{P}(\Gamma)
. Then
\widehat{\varphi}\in L^{1}({\Gamma}^{\wedge})
by the Fourier Inversion Theorem (see
[
51
, page 22]
) and
\widehat{\varphi}
is positive by
[
23
, Corollary 4.23]
.

Let now
\mu_{\varphi}
be the non-negative and bounded (as
\varphi\in L^{1}(\Gamma)^{+}
) regular measure defined on a generic Borel subset
E
of
\Gamma
by
\mu_{\varphi}(E)=\int_{x\in E}\varphi(x)\mathrm{d}\mu(x)
. According to
[
51
, §1.3.4]
, for every
\gamma\in{\Gamma}^{\wedge}
,

\widehat{\varphi}(\gamma)=\int_{x\in\Gamma}\gamma(-x)\mathrm{d}\mu_{\varphi}(x)={\int_{x\in\Gamma}\gamma(x)^{-1}\mathrm{d}\mu_{\varphi}(x)}=\int_{x\in\Gamma}\gamma^{-1}(x)\mathrm{d}\mu_{\varphi}(x).

Trading
\gamma
for
\gamma^{-1}
gives
(\widehat{\varphi})^{\star}(\gamma)=\widehat{\varphi}(\gamma^{-1})=\int_{x\in\Gamma}\gamma(x)\mathrm{d}\mu_{\varphi}(x).

By the Bochner Theorem (see
[
51
, page 19]
),
({\widehat{\varphi}})^{\star}\in\mathfrak{P}({\Gamma}^{\wedge})
. By Lemma
A.2
, this proves that
{\widehat{\varphi}}\in\mathfrak{P}({\Gamma}^{\wedge})
.
∎

As a consequence of the above result, and in particular of the fact that
\widehat{\varphi}
is positive and positive-definite, we get that
\widehat{\varphi}
is symmetric (i.e.,
\widehat{\varphi}=({\widehat{\varphi}})^{\star}
) by Lemma
A.2
(2).

A proof of the following fact can be found in
[
32
, Theorem (32.9)]
, but we offer here a self-contained proof for the sake of completeness, since we use it in Lemma
A.11
.

Lemma A.8
.

If
\varphi,\psi\in L^{1}(\Gamma)
are positive-definite, then
\varphi\cdot\psi
is positive-definite.

Proof.

By the Bochner Theorem, there exists non-negative measures
\mu
and
\nu
on
{\Gamma}^{\wedge}
such that, for every
x\in\Gamma
,

\varphi(x)=\int_{\gamma\in{\Gamma}^{\wedge}}\gamma(x)\mathrm{d}\mu(\gamma)\ \mbox{ and }\ \psi(x)=\int_{\gamma\in{\Gamma}^{\wedge}}\gamma(x)\mathrm{d}\nu(\gamma).

(A.1)

After multiplication in (
A.1
) one obtains

\varphi(x)\psi(x)=\int_{\gamma\in{\Gamma}^{\wedge}}\gamma(x)\mathrm{d}\mu(\gamma)\int_{\gamma^{\prime}\in{\Gamma}^{\wedge}}\gamma^{\prime}(x)\mathrm{d}\nu(\gamma^{\prime})=\int_{\gamma\in{\Gamma}^{\wedge}}\left(\int_{\gamma^{\prime}\in{\Gamma}^{\wedge}}\gamma(x)\gamma^{\prime}(x)\mathrm{d}\nu(\gamma^{\prime})\right)\mathrm{d}\mu(\gamma)=\int_{\gamma\in{\Gamma}^{\wedge}}\left(\int_{\gamma^{\prime}\in{\Gamma}^{\wedge}}(\gamma\cdot\gamma^{\prime})(x)\mathrm{d}\nu(\gamma^{\prime})\right)\mathrm{d}\mu(\gamma).

This chain of equalities can extend by making use of the standard topological isomorphism
\omega_{\Gamma}\colon\Gamma\to\Gamma^{\wedge\wedge}
:

\int_{\gamma\in{\Gamma}^{\wedge}}\left(\int_{\gamma^{\prime}\in{\Gamma}^{\wedge}}(\gamma\cdot\gamma^{\prime})(x)\mathrm{d}\nu(\gamma^{\prime})\right)\mathrm{d}\mu(\gamma)=\int_{\gamma\in{\Gamma}^{\wedge}}\left(\int_{\gamma^{\prime}\in{\Gamma}^{\wedge}}\omega_{\Gamma}(x)(\gamma\cdot\gamma^{\prime}))\mathrm{d}\nu(\gamma^{\prime})\right)\mathrm{d}\mu(\gamma).

At this point we can make use of the convolution
\mu*\nu
, that is again a non-negative measure (see equation (2) from
[
51
, §1.3.1]
), which allows us to write (see (4) from
[
51
, §1.3.1]
)

\int_{\gamma\in{\Gamma}^{\wedge}}\left(\int_{\gamma^{\prime}\in{\Gamma}^{\wedge}}\omega_{\Gamma}(x)(\gamma\cdot\gamma^{\prime}))\mathrm{d}\nu(\gamma^{\prime})\right)\mathrm{d}\mu(\gamma)=\int_{\gamma\in{\Gamma}^{\wedge}}\omega_{\Gamma}(x)(\gamma)\mathrm{d}(\mu*\nu)(\gamma)=\int_{\gamma\in\Gamma}\gamma(x)\mathrm{d}(\mu*\nu)(\gamma).

This gives, for every
x\in\Gamma
,

(\varphi\cdot\psi)(x)=\int_{\gamma\in{\Gamma}^{\wedge}}\gamma(x)\mathrm{d}(\mu*\nu)(\gamma).

From the opposite implication of the Bochner Theorem we deduce that that
\varphi\cdot\psi
is positive-definite.
∎

Next we see how the Fourier transform behaves with respect to product and convolution.

Lemma A.9
.

Let
\varphi,\psi\in L^{1}(\Gamma)
. Then:

(1)

\widehat{\varphi*\psi}=\widehat{\varphi}\cdot\widehat{\psi}
;

(2)

\widehat{\varphi\cdot\psi}=\widehat{\varphi}*\widehat{\psi}
if moreover
\varphi,\psi\in L^{2}(\Gamma)
.

Proof.

(1) is
[
51
, Theorem 1.2.4(a),(b)]
and (2) is
[
23
, (4.36)]
.
∎

Finally, in our case of interest the Fourier transform is involutive:

Lemma A.10
.

If
\varphi\in L^{1}(\Gamma)\cap\mathfrak{P}(\Gamma)^{+}
, then
\widehat{\widehat{\varphi}}=\varphi
.

Proof.

By Lemma
A.2
(2),
\varphi^{\star}=\varphi
. Hence,
\widehat{\widehat{\varphi}}=\varphi^{\star}=\varphi
, by
[
23
, (4.32)]
.
∎

A.2
Algebraic and topological Peters monoids

For the compact Abelian group
K
, denote by
0\colon K\to\mathbb{C}
the
0
function and define the
topological Peters monoid

\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{top}}(K)=(L^{1}(K)^{+}\cap\mathfrak{P}(K))\setminus\{0\},\quad\text{with}\ w_{\mathrm{top}}\colon\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{top}}(K)\to{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0},\ \text{such that}\ w_{\mathrm{top}}(\varphi)=\log\frac{\varphi(0)}{\|\varphi\|_{1}}.

The definition of
w_{\mathrm{top}}
is correct since
\varphi(0)\geq\|\varphi\|_{1}
by Lemma
A.2
(3) and
\|\varphi\|_{1}\neq 0
, having excluded those
\varphi
that are
0
almost everywhere.

Lemma A.11
.

In the above notation, the following statements hold true:

(1)

\widehat{\varphi\cdot\psi}=\widehat{\varphi}*\widehat{\psi}
for
\varphi,\psi\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{top}}(K)
;

(2)

(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{top}}(K),\cdot)
is a commutative monoid, with unit
\chi_{K}
;

(3)

the norm
w_{\mathrm{top}}\colon\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{top}}(K)\to{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}
is monotone.

Proof.

(1) Since
\varphi,\psi
are continuous (hence,
\varphi,\psi\in L^{2}(K)
), it is enough to apply Lemma
A.9
(2).

(2) Assume, that
\varphi,\psi\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{top}}(K)
. Clearly, they commute as they are complex-valued functions. Moreover,
\varphi
and
\psi
are non-zero continuous function in
L^{1}(K)^{+}
, and hence,
\varphi\cdot\psi
is continuous, so
\varphi\cdot\psi\in L^{1}(K)^{+}
. Finally,
\varphi\cdot\psi
is positive-definite by Lemma
A.8
.

(3) Let
\varphi,\psi\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{top}}(K)
. For every
x\in K
,
|\varphi(x)\psi(x)|=\varphi(x)\psi(x)\leq\varphi(x)\psi(0)
, by Lemma
A.2
(1). Then, we have that
\|\varphi\cdot\psi\|_{1}\leq\|\varphi\|_{1}\psi(0)
, and so

\frac{\varphi(0)}{\|\varphi\|_{1}}\leq\frac{\varphi(0)\psi(0)}{\|\varphi\cdot\psi\|_{1}}=\frac{(\varphi\cdot\psi)(0)}{\|\varphi\cdot\psi\|_{1}},

which gives
w_{\mathrm{top}}(\varphi)\leq w_{\mathrm{top}}(\varphi\cdot\psi)
.
∎

Dually, for the discrete Abelian group
X
, denote by
0\colon X\to\mathbb{C}
the
0
-function and define the
algebraic Peters monoid

\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{alg}}(X)=(L^{1}(X)^{+}\cap\mathfrak{P}(X))\setminus\{0\},\quad\text{with}\ w_{\mathrm{alg}}\colon\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{alg}}(X)\to{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0},\ \text{such that}\ w_{\mathrm{alg}}(\varphi)=\log\frac{\|\varphi\|_{1}}{\varphi(0)}.

This makes sense since
\|\varphi\|_{1}=\sum_{x\in X}\varphi(x)\geq\varphi(0)\neq 0
.

Lemma A.12
.

In the above notation, the following statements hold true:

(1)

if
\varphi\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{alg}}(X)
(resp.,
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{top}}(K)
), then
\widehat{\varphi}\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{top}}(K)
(resp.,
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{alg}}(X)
);

(2)

(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{alg}}(X),*)
is a commutative monoid, with unit
\chi_{\{0\}}
;

(3)

the norm
w_{\mathrm{alg}}\colon\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{alg}}(X)\to{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}
is monotone.

Proof.

(1) follows from Lemma
A.7
.

(2) Assume, that
\varphi,\psi\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{alg}}(X)
. Then
\varphi*\psi\in L^{1}(X)^{+}
, by Lemma
A.4
(2), and
\varphi*\psi=\psi*\varphi
by Lemma
A.3
(1).

It remains to check that
\varphi*\psi
is positive-definite. By Lemma
A.10
,
\widehat{\widehat{\varphi}}=\varphi
and
\widehat{\widehat{\psi}}=\psi
.
This gives
\varphi*\psi=\widehat{\widehat{\varphi}}*\widehat{\widehat{\psi}}=\widehat{\widehat{\varphi}\cdot\widehat{\psi}}
, by Lemma
A.11
(1), using the fact that
\widehat{\varphi},\widehat{\psi}\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{top}}(K)
by (1). Since
\widehat{\varphi}\cdot\widehat{\psi}\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{top}}(K)
, by Lemma
A.11
(2), using again item (1), we deduce that
\varphi*\psi=\widehat{\widehat{\varphi}\cdot\widehat{\psi}}\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{alg}}(X)
.

(3) For
\varphi,\psi\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{alg}}(X)
, using Lemma
A.4
(2) and (4), we get

\frac{\|\varphi*\psi\|_{1}}{(\varphi*\psi)(0)}\geq\frac{\|\varphi\|_{1}\|\psi\|_{1}}{\varphi(0)\|\psi\|_{1}}=\frac{\|\varphi\|_{1}}{\varphi(0)},

that is,
w_{\mathrm{alg}}(\varphi*\psi)\geq w_{\mathrm{alg}}(\varphi)
.
∎

Now we are in position to prove the following fundamental isomorphism between the algebraic and the topological Peters monoid. From now on,
K={X}^{\wedge}
.

Theorem A.13
.

In the above setting,
\widehat{(-)}\colon\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{alg}}(X)\to\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{top}}(K)
, such that
\varphi\mapsto\widehat{\varphi}

is an isomorphism of normed monoids. In particular,
\widehat{\chi_{\{0\}}}=\chi_{K}
,
\widehat{\varphi*\psi}=\widehat{\varphi}\cdot\widehat{\psi}
and
w_{\mathrm{alg}}(\varphi)=w_{\mathrm{top}}(\widehat{\varphi})
, for all
\varphi,\psi\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{alg}}(X)
.

Proof.

By Lemma
A.12
(1), the function is well-defined, and by Lemma
A.9
(1), it is a homomorphism of monoids.
Moreover, for every
\varphi\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{alg}}(X)
,
w_{\mathrm{alg}}(\varphi)=w_{\mathrm{top}}(\widehat{\varphi})
, as

\displaystyle\widehat{\varphi}(0_{K})=\int_{x\in X}\varphi(x)\overline{0_{K}(x)}\mathrm{d}\mu(x)=\int_{x\in X}\varphi(x)\mathrm{d}\mu(x)=\|\varphi\|_{1},

\displaystyle\|{\widehat{\text{}\varphi}}\|_{1}=\int_{x\in K}\widehat{\varphi}(x)\mathrm{d}\mu(x)=\int_{x\in K}\widehat{\varphi}(x)\overline{0_{{K}^{\wedge}}(x)}\mathrm{d}\mu(x)=\widehat{\widehat{\text{}\varphi}}({0_{K^{\wedge}}})=\varphi({0_{X}}),

where we use Lemma
A.10
for the last equality.
Since
\varphi=\widehat{\widehat{\varphi}}
by Lemma
A.10
, and
\widehat{\varphi}\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{alg}}({K}^{\wedge})
by Lemma
A.12
(1), we conclude that
\widehat{(-)}\colon\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{alg}}(X)\to\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{top}}(K)
has
\widehat{(-)}\colon\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{top}}(K)\to\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{alg}}({K}^{\wedge})=\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{alg}}(X)
as inverse function and so it is bijective.
∎

A.2.1
Transfering the actions to Peters monoids

A given automorphism
\alpha\colon X\to X
of the discrete Abelian group
X
induces a map:

(-)\circ\alpha^{-1}\colon\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{alg}}(X)\to\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{alg}}(X),\quad\text{such that}\quad\varphi\mapsto\varphi\circ\alpha^{-1}.

It is not difficult to verify the properties in the following lemma, showing that
(-)\circ\alpha^{-1}
is an isomorphism of normed monoids. In item (4) the need to take
C
symmetric, comes from the fact that for a
C\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{fin}}(X)
one has
\chi_{C}
and/or
\chi_{\alpha(C)}\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{alg}}(X)
precisely when
C\in\mathfrak{F}(X)
.

Lemma A.14
.

Given an automorphism
\alpha\colon X\to X
of the discrete Abelian group
X
and
\varphi,\psi\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{alg}}(X)
,

(1)

\varphi\circ\alpha^{-1}\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{alg}}(X)
;

(2)

\|\varphi\circ\alpha^{-1}\|_{1}=\|\varphi\|_{1}
and
(\varphi\circ\alpha^{-1})(0)=\varphi(0)
;

(3)

(\varphi*\psi)\circ\alpha^{-1}=(\varphi\circ\alpha^{-1})*(\psi\circ\alpha^{-1})
and
\chi_{\{0\}}\circ\alpha^{-1}=\chi_{\{0\}}
;

(4)

if
C\in{\mathfrak{F}}(X)
, then
\chi_{\alpha(C)}=\chi_{C}\circ\alpha^{-1}
and so
\chi_{\alpha(C)}*\chi_{\alpha(C)}=(\chi_{C}*\chi_{C})\circ{\alpha^{-1}}
.

The following left
G
-action is well defined by Lemma
A.14
:

G\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 6.0pt$\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}}$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowright\ $}}\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{alg}}(X),\quad\text{such that}\ \ (\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}})_{g}(\varphi)=\varphi\circ\lambda_{g}^{-1},\ \text{for all}\ \varphi\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{alg}}(X),g\in G.

Dually, a topological automorphism
\alpha\colon K\to K
of the compact Abelian group
K
induces a map

(-)\circ\alpha\colon\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{top}}(K)\to\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{top}}(K),\quad\text{such that}\quad\varphi\mapsto\varphi\circ\alpha.

It is not hard to check that
(-)\circ\alpha
is an isomorphism of normed monoids:

Lemma A.15
.

Given a topological automorphism
\alpha\colon K\to K
of the compact Abelian group
K
and
\varphi,\psi\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{top}}(K)
,

(1)

\varphi\circ\alpha\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{top}}(K)
;

(2)

\|\varphi\circ\alpha\|_{1}=\|\varphi\|_{1}
and
(\varphi\circ\alpha)(0)=\varphi(0)
;

(3)

(\varphi\cdot\psi)\circ\alpha=(\varphi\circ\alpha)\cdot(\psi\circ\alpha)
and
\chi_{K}\circ\alpha=\chi_{K}
;

(4)

if
U\in{\mathfrak{U}}(K)
, then
\chi_{\alpha^{-1}(U)}=\chi_{U}\circ\alpha
.

The following left
G
-action is well-defined by Lemma
A.15
:

G\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 6.0pt$\rho_{\mathrm{top}}$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowright\ $}}\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{top}}(K),\quad\text{such that}\ \ (\rho_{\mathrm{top}})_{g}(\varphi)=\varphi\circ\rho_{g},\ \text{for all}\ \varphi\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{top}}(X),g\in G.

Proposition A.16
.

The left
G
-actions
G\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 6.0pt$\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}}$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowright\ $}}\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{alg}}(X)
and
G\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 6.0pt$\rho_{\mathrm{top}}$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowright\ $}}\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{top}}(K)
are conjugated via the isomorphism of normed monoids induced by the Fourier transform
f=\widehat{(-)}\colon\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{alg}}(X)\to\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{top}}(K)
. Thus,
h(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},{\mathfrak{s}}))=h(\rho_{\mathrm{top}},{\mathfrak{s}})
for any right Følner net
{\mathfrak{s}}
for
G
.

Proof.

We have to verify the formula
f\circ(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}})_{g}=(\rho_{\mathrm{top}})_{g}\circ f
, for all
g\in G
. Indeed, fix
g\in G
and
\varphi\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{alg}}(X)
, then

(f\circ(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}})_{g})(\varphi)=\widehat{\varphi\circ\lambda_{g}^{-1}}
and
((\rho_{\mathrm{top}})_{g}\circ f)(\varphi)=\widehat{\varphi}\circ\rho_{g}
. Now, for
\gamma\in K={X}^{\wedge}
, since
\rho_{g}={(\lambda_{g})}^{\wedge}
,

\begin{split}\widehat{\varphi\circ\lambda_{g}^{-1}}(\gamma)&=((\varphi\circ\lambda_{g}^{-1})*\gamma)(0)=\sum_{x\in X}\varphi(\lambda_{g}^{-1}(x))\gamma(x)=\\
&=\sum_{x\in X}\varphi(x)\gamma(\lambda_{g}(x))=(\varphi*(\gamma\circ\lambda_{g}))(0)=(\varphi*\rho_{g}(\gamma))(0)=(\widehat{\varphi}\circ\rho_{g})(\gamma),\end{split}

and this gives the required equality.
The last assertion follows from Proposition
1.16
.
∎

A.3
Approximating the algebraic side

The goal of this subsection is to prove that the left
G
-actions
G\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 6.0pt$\lambda_{\mathfrak{F}}$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowright\ $}}{\mathfrak{F}}(X)
(see Example
1.19
) and
G\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 6.0pt$\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}}$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowright\ $}}\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{alg}}(X)
are asymptotically equivalent.

We use several times the following immediate consequence of Lemmas
A.4
(2) and
A.14
(2).

Corollary A.17
.

For every
\varphi\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{alg}}(X)
and
F\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{fin}}(G)
,
\|T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\varphi)\|_{1}=\|\varphi\|_{1}^{|F|}
and
\|T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\varphi*\varphi)\|_{1}=\|\varphi\|^{2|F|}_{1}
.

Proposition A.18
.

G\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 6.0pt$\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}}$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowright\ $}}\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{alg}}(X)
asymptotically dominates
G\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 6.0pt$\lambda_{\mathfrak{F}}$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowright\ $}}{\mathfrak{F}}(X)
.

Proof.

Given
H\in{\mathfrak{F}}(X)
, let
H^{(n)}=\{h_{1}+\ldots+h_{n}:h_{j}\in H,{j=1,\ldots,n}\}
for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}_{+}
, and define

\varphi_{n}=\chi_{H^{(n)}}*\chi_{H^{(n)}}\quad\text{and}\quad\varepsilon_{n}=2\cdot\log\frac{|H^{(n+1)}|}{|H^{(n)}|}.

As Abelian groups have polynomial growth,
\{\varepsilon_{n}\}_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}_{+}}
converges to
0
. Choose
n\in{\mathbb{N}}_{+}
and
F=\{f_{1},\dots,f_{k}\}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{fin}}(G)
.
First, with Lemma
A.14
(3) and (4), compute

\displaystyle T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\varphi_{n})

\displaystyle=(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}})_{f_{1}}(\varphi_{n})*\ldots*(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}})_{f_{k}}(\varphi_{n})

\displaystyle=(\varphi_{n}\circ\lambda_{f_{1}}^{-1})*\ldots*(\varphi_{n}\circ\lambda_{f_{k}}^{-1})

\displaystyle=((\chi_{H^{(n)}}*\chi_{H^{(n)}})\circ\lambda_{f_{1}}^{-1})*\ldots*((\chi_{H^{(n)}}*\chi_{H^{(n)}})\circ\lambda_{f_{k}}^{-1})

\displaystyle=((\chi_{H^{(n)}}\circ\lambda_{f_{1}}^{-1})*(\chi_{H^{(n)}}\circ\lambda_{f_{1}}^{-1}))*\ldots*((\chi_{H^{(n)}}\circ\lambda_{f_{k}}^{-1})*(\chi_{H^{(n)}}\circ\lambda_{f_{k}}^{-1}))

\displaystyle=(\chi_{\lambda_{f_{1}}(H^{(n)})})*(\chi_{\lambda_{f_{1}}(H^{(n)})})*\ldots*(\chi_{\lambda_{f_{k}}(H^{(n)})})*(\chi_{\lambda_{f_{k}}(H^{(n)})});

and also, by Corollaries
A.17
and
A.6
(2),

\|T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\varphi_{n})\|_{1}=|H^{(n)}|^{2|F|}.

(A.2)

Given
x\in T_{F}(\lambda,H^{(2)})
, write
x=\sum_{j=1}^{k}(h_{j}+h_{j}^{\prime})
with
h_{j},h_{j}^{\prime}\in\lambda_{f_{j}}(H)
. By Lemmas
A.3
(2) and
A.14
(3) and (4),

\displaystyle T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\varphi_{n+1})_{x}

\displaystyle=((\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}})_{f_{1}}(\varphi_{n+1}))_{h_{1}+h^{\prime}_{1}}*\ldots*((\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}})_{f_{k}}(\varphi_{n+1}))_{h_{k}+h^{\prime}_{k}}

(A.3)

\displaystyle=(\chi_{\lambda_{f_{1}}(H^{(n+1)})})_{h_{1}}*(\chi_{\lambda_{f_{1}}(H^{(n+1)})})_{h^{\prime}_{1}}*\ldots*(\chi_{\lambda_{f_{k}}(H^{(n+1)})})_{h_{k}}*(\chi_{\lambda_{f_{k}}(H^{(n+1)})})_{h^{\prime}_{k}}.

Since
(\chi_{\lambda_{f}(H^{(n+1)})})_{h}\geq\chi_{\lambda_{f}(H^{(n)})}
, for all
f\in F
and
h\in\lambda_{f}(H)
, we deduce, with Lemma
A.4
(5), that

(\chi_{\lambda_{f_{1}}(H^{(n+1)})})_{h_{1}}*(\chi_{\lambda_{f_{1}}(H^{(n+1)})})_{h^{\prime}_{1}}*\ldots*(\chi_{\lambda_{f_{k}}(H^{(n+1)})})_{h_{k}}*(\chi_{\lambda_{f_{k}}(H^{(n+1)})})_{h^{\prime}_{k}}\!\geq\!(\chi_{\lambda_{f_{1}}(H^{(n)})})*(\chi_{\lambda_{f_{1}}(H^{(n)})})*\ldots*(\chi_{\lambda_{f_{k}}(H^{(n)})})*(\chi_{\lambda_{f_{k}}(H^{(n)})})\!=\!T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\varphi_{n}).

Using (
A.3
) and the above inequality, after evaluation at
0
, we get

T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\varphi_{n+1})(x)=T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\varphi_{n+1})_{x}(0)\geq T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\varphi_{n})(0).

(A.4)

Hence,

\|T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\varphi_{n+1})\|_{1}=\sum_{x\in X}T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\varphi_{n+1})(x)\overset{(*)}{\geq}\sum_{x\in T_{F}(\lambda,H^{(2)})}T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\varphi_{n+1})(x)\overset{(**)}{\geq}|T_{F}(\lambda,H^{(2)})|\cdot T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\varphi_{n})(0),

where
(*)
follows as we are shrinking the indexing family, while
(**)
follows by (
A.4
). We conclude that:

|T_{F}(\lambda,H^{(2)})|\leq\frac{\|T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\varphi_{n+1})\|_{1}}{T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\varphi_{n})(0)}=\frac{\|T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\varphi_{n})\|_{1}}{T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\varphi_{n})(0)}\cdot\frac{\|T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\varphi_{n+1})\|_{1}}{\|T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\varphi_{n})\|_{1}}=\frac{\|T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\varphi_{n})\|_{1}}{T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\varphi_{n})(0)}\cdot\left(\frac{|H^{(n+1)}|}{|H^{(n)}|}\right)^{2|F|},

where the last equality follows from (
A.2
). Taking logarithms, one gets the desired inequality:

\log|T_{F}(\lambda,H)|\leq\log|T_{F}(\lambda,H^{(2)})|\leq w_{\mathrm{alg}}(T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\varphi_{n}))+|F|\cdot\varepsilon_{n}.\qed

The converse of Proposition
A.18
is technically a bit more challenging, as it relies on a series of successive reductions, that correspond to the various parts of the following lemma:

Lemma A.19
.

Let
\varphi\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{alg}}(X)
and
F\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{fin}}(G)
. Then:

(1)

w_{\mathrm{alg}}(T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\varphi))\leq w_{\mathrm{alg}}(T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\varphi*\varphi))
;

(2)

for
\psi=\frac{\varphi}{\|\varphi\|_{1}}
,
\|\psi\|_{1}=1
and
w_{\mathrm{alg}}(T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\varphi))=w_{\mathrm{alg}}(T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\psi))
;

(3)

given
n\in{\mathbb{N}}_{+}
, there is
H\in{\mathfrak{F}}(X)
such that
\|\varphi-\varphi\cdot\chi_{H}\|_{1}\leq\frac{1}{2n}
;

(4)

suppose that
\|\varphi\|_{1}=1
, take
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
with
n>1
, and let
\xi=\varphi\cdot\chi_{H}
be as in (3), then
\xi*\xi\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{alg}}(X)
and

w_{\mathrm{alg}}(T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\varphi*\varphi))\leq w_{\mathrm{alg}}(T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\xi*\xi))+|F|\log\frac{n}{n-1}.

Proof.

(1) By Corollary
A.17
,
\|T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\varphi*\varphi)\|_{1}=\|\varphi\|^{2|F|}_{1}
. Furthermore,

\displaystyle T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\varphi*\varphi)(0)

\displaystyle=\sum_{x\in X}T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\varphi)(x)\cdot T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\varphi)(-x)\overset{(*)}{\leq}\sum_{x\in X}T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\varphi)(x)\cdot T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\varphi)(0)

\displaystyle=\|T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\varphi)\|_{1}\cdot T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\varphi)(0)=\|\varphi\|^{|F|}_{1}\cdot T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\varphi)(0),

where
(*)
holds by Lemma
A.2
(1).
Using both equalities in Corollary
A.17
and the above computation, we get:

\frac{\|T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\varphi)\|_{1}}{T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\varphi)(0)}=\frac{\|\varphi\|_{1}^{|F|}}{T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\varphi)(0)}=\frac{\|\varphi\|_{1}^{2|F|}}{\|\varphi\|_{1}^{|F|}\cdot T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\varphi)(0)}\leq\frac{\|T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\varphi*\varphi)\|_{1}}{T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\varphi*\varphi)(0)}.

(2) Let
\varepsilon=\|\varphi\|_{1}^{-1}\in{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
. Then

\|\psi\|_{1}=\sum_{x\in X}\psi(x)=\varepsilon\cdot\sum_{x\in X}\varphi(x)=\varepsilon\cdot\|\varphi\|_{1}=1
. Similarly, if
F=\{f_{1},\dots,f_{k}\}
, then

T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\psi)(0)=(\psi^{\lambda_{f_{1}}}*\ldots*\psi^{\lambda_{f_{k}}})(0)=\varepsilon^{k}\cdot(\varphi^{\lambda_{f_{1}}}*\ldots*\varphi^{\lambda_{f_{k}}})(0)=\varepsilon^{k}\cdot T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\varphi)(0).

Now, by Corollary
A.17
,
\|T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\psi)\|_{1}=\|\psi\|_{1}^{k}=1
and
\|T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\varphi)\|_{1}=\|\varphi\|_{1}^{k}=\varepsilon^{-k}
.
Combining the above computations we can easily conclude:

w_{\mathrm{alg}}(T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\varphi))=\frac{\|\varphi\|_{1}^{k}}{T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\varphi)(0)}=\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{k}\cdot T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\varphi)(0)}=\frac{1}{T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\psi)(0)}=w_{\mathrm{alg}}(T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\psi)).

(3) follows since
\|\varphi\|_{1}=\sum_{x\in X}\varphi(x)=\sup\{\sum_{x\in H}\varphi(x):H\in{\mathfrak{F}}(X)\}<\infty
.

(4) Since
\xi\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{alg}}(X)
by definition, also
\xi*\xi\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{alg}}(X)
. Furthermore, by the triangular inequality and Lemma
A.4
(2) and (5),

\displaystyle\|\xi*\xi-\varphi*\varphi\|_{1}

\displaystyle=\|\xi*\xi-\xi*\varphi+\xi*\varphi-\varphi*\varphi\|_{1}\leq\|\xi*\xi-\xi*\varphi\|_{1}+\|\xi*\varphi-\varphi*\varphi\|_{1}\leq

\displaystyle\leq\|\xi\|_{1}\|\xi-\varphi\|_{1}+\|\varphi\|_{1}\|\xi-\varphi\|_{1}\overset{(*)}{\leq}2\|\xi-\varphi\|_{1}<\frac{1}{n},

where
(*)
follows since
\|\xi\|_{1}\leq\|\varphi\|_{1}=1
.
As
\|\varphi*\varphi\|_{1}=\|\varphi\|_{1}^{2}=1
by Lemma
A.4
(2), we get

\|\varphi*\varphi\|_{1}-\|\xi*\xi-\varphi*\varphi\|_{1}\geq 1-\frac{1}{n}=\frac{n-1}{n}.

(A.5)

Furthermore, the fact that
\xi\leq\varphi
implies that
\xi*\xi\leq\varphi*\varphi
by Lemma
A.4
(5), and so
(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}})_{g}(\xi*\xi)\leq(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}})_{g}(\varphi*\varphi)
for each
g\in G
; thus,
T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\xi*\xi)(0)\leq T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\varphi*\varphi)(0)
. Using the latter inequality, (
A.5
), Corollary
A.17
(to see that
\|T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\xi*\xi)\|_{1}=\|\xi*\xi\|^{|F|}_{1}
) and that
\|T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\varphi*\varphi)(0)\|_{1}=\|\varphi\|_{1}^{2|F|}=1
by Corollary
A.17
, we deduce that:

\begin{split}\frac{\|T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\xi*\xi)\|_{1}}{T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\xi*\xi)(0)}&\geq\frac{\|\xi*\xi\|^{|F|}_{1}}{T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\varphi*\varphi)(0)}\geq\frac{(\|\varphi*\varphi\|_{1}-\|\xi*\xi-\varphi*\varphi\|_{1})^{|F|}}{T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\varphi*\varphi)(0)}>\\
&>\frac{((n-1)/n)^{|F|}}{T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\varphi*\varphi)(0)}=\frac{\|T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\varphi*\varphi)(0)\|_{1}}{T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\varphi*\varphi)(0)}\cdot\left(\frac{n-1}{n}\right)^{|F|}.\end{split}

To conclude, divide both sides by
((n-1)/n)^{|F|}
and apply logarithms.
∎

Proposition A.20
.

G\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 6.0pt$\lambda_{\mathfrak{F}}$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowright\ $}}{\mathfrak{F}}(X)
asymptotically dominates
G\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 6.0pt$\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}}$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowright\ $}}\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{alg}}(X)
.

Proof.

Given
\varphi\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{alg}}(X)
, let
\psi=\frac{\varphi}{\|\varphi\|_{1}}
and choose, for each
1<n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
H_{n}\in{\mathfrak{F}}(X)
such that
\|\psi-\psi\cdot\chi_{H_{n}}\|_{1}<1/(2n)
(we can do that by Lemma
A.19
(3)). Letting
\psi_{n}=\psi\cdot\chi_{H_{n}}
, we have that
\psi_{n}*\psi_{n}
has finite support by Lemma
A.3
(3). Choose
E_{n}\in{\mathfrak{F}}(X)
containing
\mathrm{supp}(\psi_{n}*\psi_{n})
, set
\varepsilon_{n}=\log|n/(n-1)|
, and let us verify that, for each
F\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{fin}}(G)
,

w_{\mathrm{alg}}(T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\varphi))\leq\log|T_{F}(\lambda,E_{n})|+|F|\cdot\varepsilon_{n}.

Using Lemma
A.19
(2), (1) and (4), we can simplify this as follows

w_{\mathrm{alg}}(T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\varphi))=w_{\mathrm{alg}}(T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\psi)\leq w_{\mathrm{alg}}(T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\psi*\psi))\leq w_{\mathrm{alg}}(T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\psi_{n}*\psi_{n}))+|F|\cdot\varepsilon_{n}.

Hence, we are left with the proof of the following inequality:

w_{\mathrm{alg}}(T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\psi_{n}*\psi_{n}))\leq\log|T_{F}(\lambda,E_{n})|.

(A.6)

For this, consider
\chi_{T_{F}(\lambda,E_{n})}*T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\psi_{n}*\psi_{n})(0)
. Then,

\chi_{T_{F}(\lambda,E_{n})}*T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\psi_{n}*\psi_{n})(0)=\sum_{x\in X}\chi_{T_{F}(\lambda,E_{n})}(x)\cdot T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\psi_{n}*\psi_{n})(-x)=\|T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\psi_{n}*\psi_{n})\|_{1},

since
\mathrm{supp}(T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\psi_{n}*\psi_{n}))\subseteq T_{F}(\lambda,E_{n})
, which is symmetric. Furthermore, by Lemma
A.2
(2),

\chi_{T_{F}(\lambda,E_{n})}*T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\psi_{n}*\psi_{n})(0)=\sum_{x\in X}\chi_{T_{F}(\lambda,E_{n})}(x)\cdot T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\psi_{n}*\psi_{n})(-x)\leq|T_{F}(\lambda,E_{n})|T_{F}(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},\psi_{n}*\psi_{n})(0).\spadesuit

Now (
A.6
) is obtained by combining the above computations and taking logarithms.
∎

The above two propositions give immediately the following:

Corollary A.21
.

G\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 6.0pt$\lambda_{\mathfrak{F}}$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowright\ $}}{\mathfrak{F}}(X)
and
G\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 6.0pt$\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}}$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowright\ $}}\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{alg}}(X)
are asymptotically equivalent.
Hence,
h(\lambda_{\mathfrak{F}},{\mathfrak{s}})=h(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},{\mathfrak{s}})
for every right Følner net
{\mathfrak{s}}
for
G
.

A.4
Approximating the topological side

The goal of this subsection is to verify the asymptotic equivalence of two pairs of left
G
-actions: first we show that
G\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 6.0pt$\rho_{\mathrm{top}}$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowright\ $}}\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{top}}(K)
and
G\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 6.0pt$\rho_{\mathfrak{U}}$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowright\ $}}{\mathfrak{U}}(K)
(see Example
1.24
) are asymptotically equivalent and, after that, we show that
G\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 6.0pt$\rho_{\mathfrak{U}}$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowright\ $}}{\mathfrak{U}}(K)
and
G\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 6.0pt$\rho_{\mathrm{cov}}$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowright\ $}}{\mathrm{cov}}(K)
(see Example
1.23
) are equivalent.

Proposition A.22
.

G\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 6.0pt$\rho_{\mathfrak{U}}$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowright\ $}}{\mathfrak{U}}(K)
and
G\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 6.0pt$\rho_{\mathrm{top}}$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowright\ $}}\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{top}}(K)
are asymptotically equivalent.
Hence,
h(\rho_{\mathfrak{U}},{\mathfrak{s}})=h(\rho_{\mathrm{top}},{\mathfrak{s}})
for every right Følner net
{\mathfrak{s}}
for
G
.

Proof.

We show first that
G\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 6.0pt$\rho_{\mathrm{top}}$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowright\ $}}\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{top}}(K)
dominates
G\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 6.0pt$\rho_{\mathfrak{U}}$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowright\ $}}{\mathfrak{U}}(K)
.
Let
U\in{\mathfrak{U}}(K)
. By Corollary
A.5
, there is
\varphi\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{top}}(K)
such that
\mathrm{supp}(\varphi)\subseteq U
and, up to rescaling, we may suppose that
\varphi(0)=1
. Then,
{\|T_{F}(\rho_{\mathrm{top}},\varphi)\|_{1}}\leq\mu(T_{F}(\rho,U))
, by Lemma
A.2
(3).
Therefore, as
T_{F}(\rho_{\mathrm{top}},\varphi)(0)=\prod_{g\in F}(\varphi\circ\rho_{g})(0)=\varphi(0)^{|F|}
,

w_{\mathrm{top}}(T_{F}(\rho_{\mathrm{top}},\varphi))=\log\left(\frac{\varphi(0)^{|F|}}{\|T_{F}(\rho_{\mathrm{top}},\varphi)\|_{1}}\right)\geq-\log\mu(T_{F}(\rho,U))=v_{\mathfrak{U}}(T_{F}(\rho,U)).

Next we check that
G\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 6.0pt$\rho_{\mathfrak{U}}$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowright\ $}}{\mathfrak{U}}(K)
asymptotically dominates
G\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 6.0pt$\rho_{\mathrm{top}}$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowright\ $}}\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{top}}(K)
.
Let
\varphi\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{top}}(K)
and set
c=\varphi(0)>0
. For each
r\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
, we let
B_{0}(r)\subseteq\mathbb{C}
be the open ball of radius
r
around
0
. Furthermore, for each
\varepsilon>0
we set:

V(\varphi,\varepsilon)=K\setminus\varphi^{-1}(B_{0}(c/(1+\varepsilon)))\subseteq K.

Since
\varphi
is continuous and each
B_{0}(r)
is open, also
\varphi^{-1}(B_{0}(r))
is open, showing that
V(\varphi,\varepsilon)
is closed (and, therefore, compact). Furthermore,
\varphi(0)\notin\mathrm{cl}(B_{0}(c/(1+\varepsilon))))
, so
V(\varphi,\varepsilon)
contains an open neighborhood of
0
.

Now, for each
g\in G
, we have that
\rho_{g}^{-1}(V(\varphi,\varepsilon))=V(\varphi\circ\rho_{g},\varepsilon)
, and so, for each
k\in K
,

\frac{(1+\varepsilon)}{\varphi(0)}\cdot(\varphi\circ\rho_{g})(k)\geq\chi_{\rho_{g}^{-1}(V(\varphi,\varepsilon))}(k),\quad\text{for every $k\in K$.}

(A.7)

For each
n\in{\mathbb{N}}_{+}
, choose
E_{n}\in{\mathfrak{U}}(K)
such that
E_{n}\subseteq V(\varphi,1/n)
. Pick an arbitrary
F\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{fin}}(G)
and let
\varepsilon=1/n
. With
g
running in
F
, multiply all inequalities (
A.7
) and apply the norm
\|-\|_{1}
to obtain:

\begin{split}\left(\frac{n+1}{n}\right)^{|F|}\frac{\|T_{F}(\rho_{\mathrm{top}},\varphi)\|_{1}}{T_{F}(\rho_{\mathrm{top}},\varphi)(0)}&=\left(\frac{n+1}{n}\right)^{|F|}\frac{\lVert\prod_{g\in F}\varphi\circ\rho_{g}\rVert_{1}}{\varphi(0)^{|F|}}\geq\\
&\geq\left\|\prod_{g\in F}\chi_{\rho_{g}^{-1}(V(\varphi,1/n))}\right\|_{1}\geq\left\|\prod_{g\in F}\chi_{\rho_{g}^{-1}(E_{n})}\right\|_{1}\!=\mu\left(\bigcap_{g\in G}\rho_{g}^{-1}(E_{n})\right)=\mu(T_{F}(\rho_{\mathfrak{U}},E_{n})).\end{split}

Let
\varepsilon_{n}=\log((n+1)/n)
. Taking logarithms we get the desired inequality
w_{\mathrm{top}}(T_{F}(\rho_{\mathrm{top}},\varphi))\leq-\log\mu(T_{F}(\rho_{\mathfrak{U}},E_{n}))+|F|\cdot\varepsilon_{n}.

The last assertion follows from Proposition
1.16
.
∎

Next we show that
G\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 6.0pt$\rho_{\mathfrak{U}}$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowright\ $}}{\mathfrak{U}}(K)
is equivalent to
G\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 6.0pt$\rho_{\mathrm{cov}}$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowright\ $}}{\mathrm{cov}}(K)
assuming that
K
is an arbitrary compact group.

Proposition A.23
.

G\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 6.0pt$\rho_{\mathfrak{U}}$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowright\ $}}{\mathfrak{U}}(K)
and
G\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 6.0pt$\rho_{\mathrm{cov}}$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowright\ $}}{\mathrm{cov}}(K)
are equivalent. Hence,
h(\rho_{\mathfrak{U}},{\mathfrak{s}})=h(\rho_{\mathrm{cov}},{\mathfrak{s}})

for every right Følner net
{\mathfrak{s}}
.

Proof.

First we show that
G\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 6.0pt$\rho_{\mathrm{cov}}$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowright\ $}}{\mathrm{cov}}(K)
dominates
G\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 6.0pt$\rho_{\mathfrak{U}}$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowright\ $}}{\mathfrak{U}}(K)
. To this end, let
U\in{\mathfrak{U}}(K)
, choose an open subset
V
of
K
such that
1\in V\subseteq V^{-1}V\subseteq U
, and consider the open cover
{\cal V}={\cal U}_{K}[V]\in\mathrm{cov}(K)
. Fix
F\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{fin}}(G)
.
By Lemma
4.7
(3),
{\cal W}=T_{F}(\rho_{\mathrm{cov}},{\cal V})
refines the even cover
{\cal U}_{K}[C_{F}(\rho,U)]
. Therefore,
K
can be covered by a family of
N({\cal W})
-many members of
{\cal W}
, each of which is contained in a translate of
C_{F}(\rho,U)
, hence
1=\mu(K)\leq N({\cal W})\cdot\mu(C_{F}(\rho,U))
. This proves that

-\log\mu(C_{F}(\rho,U))\leq\log N(T_{F}(\rho_{\mathrm{cov}},{\cal V})).

To see that
G\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 6.0pt$\rho_{\mathfrak{U}}$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowright\ $}}{\mathfrak{U}}(K)
dominates
G\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 6.0pt$\rho_{\mathrm{cov}}$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowright\ $}}{\mathrm{cov}}(K)
, let
{\cal V}\in\mathrm{cov}(K)
. By Lemma
4.7
(1), there exists an open neighborhood
W
of
1
such that
{\cal V}\preceq{\cal U}_{K}[W]
. Then, for each
F\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{fin}}(G)
,

\log N(T_{F}(\rho_{\mathrm{cov}},{\cal V}))\leq\log N(T_{F}(\rho_{\mathrm{cov}},{\cal U}_{K}[W])).

(A.8)

Choose
U\in{\mathfrak{U}}(K)
such that
UU\subseteq W
. Recall that there exists a finite subset
D
of
K
such that
\mathcal{D}=\{dC_{F}(\rho,W):d\in D\}
is a subcover of
{\cal U}_{K}[C_{F}(\rho,W)]
(by Lemma
4.7
(4)) and the union
\bigcup_{d\in D}(dC_{F}(\rho,U))
is disjoint; so,

N({\cal U}_{K}[C_{F}(\rho,W)])\leq|D|\ \mbox{ and }\ |D|\cdot\mu(C_{F}(\rho,U))\leq\mu(K)\leq 1.

This gives
N({\cal U}_{K}[C_{F}(\rho,W)])\leq\mu(C_{F}(\rho,U))^{-1}
. As
N(T_{F}(\rho_{\mathrm{cov}},{\cal U}_{K}[W]))\leq N({\cal U}_{K}[C_{F}(\rho,W)])
by Lemma
4.7
(2),
we conclude that
N(T_{F}(\rho_{\mathrm{cov}},{\cal U}_{K}[W]))\leq\mu(C_{F}(\rho,U))^{-1}
. Taking logarithms, together with (
A.8
), we get

\log N(T_{F}(\rho_{\mathrm{cov}},{\cal V}))\leq\log N(T_{F}(\rho_{\mathrm{cov}},{\cal U}_{K}[W]))\leq-\log\mu(C_{F}(\rho,U)).

This proves that
G\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 6.0pt$\rho_{\mathfrak{U}}$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowright\ $}}{\mathfrak{U}}(K)
and
G\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 6.0pt$\rho_{\mathrm{cov}}$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowright\ $}}{\mathrm{cov}}(K)
are equivalent. The last assertion follows from Proposition
1.16
.
∎

A.5
The proof of the Bridge Theorem for amenable group actions

Theorem A.24

(Bridge Theorem)
.

Let
G
be an amenable group,
K
a compact Abelian group,
X
a discrete Abelian group and let
K\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 7.0pt$\rho$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowleft\ $}}S
and
S\mathrlap{\scalebox{.73}{\raisebox{5.8125pt}{\hskip 6.0pt$\lambda$}}}{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\ \curvearrowright\ $}}X
be a right and a left
G
-action, respectively.
Then:

(1)

h_{\mathrm{top}}(\rho)=h_{\mathrm{alg}}({\rho}^{\wedge})
;

(2)

h_{\mathrm{alg}}(\lambda)=h_{\mathrm{top}}({\lambda}^{\wedge})
.

Proof.

Since (1) and (2) are equivalent, we choose to prove (2). Let
\rho={\lambda}^{\wedge}
. For a fixed right Følner net
{\mathfrak{s}}
for
G
, we have,
by Example
1.19
and Example
1.23
,

h_{\mathrm{alg}}(\lambda)=h(\lambda_{\mathfrak{F}},{\mathfrak{s}})\quad\text{and}\quad h_{\mathrm{top}}(\rho)=h(\rho_{\mathrm{cov}},{\mathfrak{s}}).

Furthermore,
h(\lambda_{\mathfrak{F}},{\mathfrak{s}})=h(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},{\mathfrak{s}})
by Corollary
A.21
,
h(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},{\mathfrak{s}})=h(\rho_{\mathrm{top}},{\mathfrak{s}})
by Proposition
A.16
,

h(\rho_{\mathrm{top}},{\mathfrak{s}})=h(\rho_{\mathfrak{U}},{\mathfrak{s}})
by Proposition
A.22
, and
h(\rho_{\mathfrak{U}},{\mathfrak{s}})=h(\rho_{\mathrm{cov}},{\mathfrak{s}})
by Proposition
A.23
.
We conclude that

h_{\mathrm{alg}}(\lambda)=h(\lambda_{\mathfrak{F}},{\mathfrak{s}})=h(\lambda_{\mathrm{alg}},{\mathfrak{s}})=h(\rho_{\mathrm{top}},{\mathfrak{s}})=h(\rho_{\mathfrak{U}},{\mathfrak{s}})=h(\rho_{\mathrm{cov}},{\mathfrak{s}})=h_{\mathrm{top}}(\rho).\qed
